(a) is
not
discussed in
(
71
)
because we consider
n
fixed while they allow
n
changing.
With Theorem
3.1
and Theorem
3.5
, we establish a clearer image on the relation between
(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
and Adam’s qualitative behavior.

Remark 7: on the divergence of SignSGD.

Theorem
3.5
implies that SignSGD (Adam with
\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}=0
) can diverge. The divergence can be avoided by modifying the algorithm, such as adding momentum
(
78
;
46
)
; or by introducing stronger assumptions like the bounded variance condition in (
2.3
) and increasing batch size
(
6
)
. In fact, the bounded variance assumption alone is sufficient to exclude our counter-example from the considered function class, and SignSGD will converge to the neighborhood of critical points with
\eta_{k}=\eta_{0}/\sqrt{k}
in that case
†
†

We have not recognized a clean convergence proof of SignSGD under bounded variance (
2.3
) without increasing batch size in the literature, but it is expected to be a simple proof. The convergence fails when relaxing (
2.3
) to our Assumption
2.2
.
. However, the analysis under bounded variance does not reveal the true divergent behavior of SignSGD. Our counter-example, which satisfies Assumption
2.2
(with
D_{1}\geq 2n^{2}
) but not bounded variance (
2.3
) (with
D_{1}=n
), provides additional motivation to relax (
2.3
). An intriguing open question is whether there exist more counter-examples with
D_{1}\in(n,2n^{2})
. We leave it for future investigation.

Remark 8: more fine-grained characterization on the critical boundary.

Integrating the divergence and convergence theories discussed above, one can conclude that there exists (at least) one critical boundary
(\beta_{1}^{*},\beta_{2}^{*})
that demarcates the di-convergence phase transition. Since both the divergence region and convergence region depend on
n
(or equivalently, depend on batch size
\frac{\mathcal{D}}{n}
), the critical boundary
(\beta_{1}^{*},\beta_{2}^{*})
must also be dependent on the batch size and is located in the white region in Fig.
1
(b). Note that our theory implies the existence of the critical boundary, whereas we have not fully determined the precise number and the shape of the boundar(-ies). Nevertheless, our experimental results in Fig.
1
(c, d) suggest that there exists only one boundary, which likely resembles the geometry of the blue region in Fig.
1
(b). We only point out its existence here and leave a more precise characterization as a future direction.

4
Key Lemmas for the Convergence Result in Theorem
3.1

Here, we summarize the key challenges in the proof of Theorem
3.1
. The proof of Theorem
3.1
can be seen in Section
7
. The proof of Theorem
3.3

follows a similar idea and we relegate the proof to Appendix
D
.

Additional notations.

For
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we use
\lceil a\rceil
to denote taking the ceiling (rounding
a
up). For
a\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote
[a]
as the index set
\{1,\cdots,a\}
. For the function indices
n
in (
1.1
), we abuse the notation
[n]=\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
.
We denote
\partial_{l}f(x)
and
\partial_{l}f_{i}(x)
as the
l
-th component of
\nabla f(x)
and
\nabla f_{i}(x)
, respectively. We use
x_{l,k}
to denote the
l
-th component of
x
at the
k
-th iteration, i.e.,
x_{k}
. Similarly for
v_{l,k}
and
m_{l,k}
. We use
\mathbb{I}(\cdot)
as the indicator function.
\mathbb{E}(\cdot)
means taking expectation over the whole trajectory.

For
k\geq 1
,
\mathbb{E}_{k}[\cdot]
denotes the conditional expectation given the entire history up to the end of iteration
k-1

(i.e., given
x_{1},m_{0},v_{0}
and all past sampled indices
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{k-1}
), but excluding the fresh sample
\tau_{k}
.

We abuse the notation of
\alpha
as follows: for
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x)
, we define
\alpha:=\arg\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x)|
; for
\partial_{\alpha}f(x)
, we define
\alpha:=\arg\max_{l}|\partial_{l}f(x)|
. Similarly for
m_{\alpha,k}
and
v_{\alpha,k}
. We use “i.i.d.” and “r.v.” as abbreviations for “independent and identically distributed” and “random variable”, respectively.

The core of the proof is to characterize when the expected update direction of Adam
\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{m_{k}}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}\right)
constitutes a descent direction, i.e., a direction that lies in the dual cone of gradient
\nabla f(x_{k})
. There are at least twofold challenges.
First
,
v_{k}
is a random variable and it appears in the denominator. This makes the entire system a stochastic non-linear dynamic system, which is difficult to analyze in general. Further,
v_{k}
can potentially hit 0, which imposes extra difficulties.
Second
,
m_{k}
contains heavy historical signals, which distort the trajectory from the gradient direction.

Figure 7:
An illustration of the changes of Adam’s update direction when
\beta_{2}
changes.

Our key insights are illustrated in Figure
7
: we find that Adam’s update direction
\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{m_{k}}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}\right)
is close to
\nabla f(x_{k})
when
\beta_{2}
is large (leading to convergence) and starts to deviate to the opposite direction when
\beta_{2}
is small (causing divergence). We now introduce the proof for our convergence results. The proof for the divergence result is shown later in Section
3.2
.

Step I: Concentration effects of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
when
\beta_{2}
is large.

We find that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
concentrates around
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}
when
\beta_{2}
is large. In particular, we prove that:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}},\text{ w.h.p., when $\beta_{2}$ is large}

With this concentration property,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
is stabilized.
Why does large
\beta_{2}
help?
Intuitively, this is because large
\beta_{2}
slows down the changes of
v_{k}
, and its behavior will become largely predictable. As a result,
-\frac{\nabla f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
becomes a descent direction in this case, i.e.,

\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\frac{\nabla f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}\right)\approx\frac{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\nabla f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})\right)}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}=\frac{\nabla f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}},

which lies in the dual cone of the gradient direction
\nabla f(x_{k})
.

It requires substantial effort to compare
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}
. In statistical theory, it is common to show the concentration of a random variable
x
around its mean
\mathbb{E}(x)
, yet it is much less common to characterize how
\frac{1}{\sqrt{x}}
deviates from
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(x)}}
, especially when
x
can be arbitrarily close to 0 and no extra boundedness condition is imposed. Without any specific property on r.v.
x
, every small deviation in
\sqrt{x}
will be amplified in
\frac{1}{\sqrt{x}}
and it can behave very badly near zero and become arbitrarily far away from
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(x)}}

(
30
)
.

In Lemma
4.1
, we prove that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
concentrates around
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}

when
\beta_{2}
is large
, and the result holds
without
any boundedness condition on stochastic gradients. We prove this result by utilizing two special properties of Adam: 1) stochastic gradients have a “geometric sum” structure in
v_{k}
. 2) the index of stochastic gradients are uniformly sampled from a finite index set. With these two special properties, we find the behavior of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
is largely predictable when
\beta_{2}
is large.

Technically, the concentration result is established via two steps: (i) map the dynamics of
1/\sqrt{v_{k}}
to the dynamics of a sequence of i.i.d. Bernoulli r.v.s, which is bounded and is much easier to analyze; (ii) map the dynamics back by several decoupling steps. Step (i) and (ii) allow us to track the dynamics of possibly unbounded r.v. sequence using bounded Bernoulli proxies. The rigorous concentration effect is shown in Lemma
4.1
as follows. Lemma
4.1
can be used as a generic tool for analyzing Adam-type algorithms.

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume Algorithm
1
satisfies:
\beta_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
;
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
;
\beta_{2}
satisfies
\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{\beta_{2}^{n}}<\frac{1}{8n}-\frac{\delta}{4}
; and
\eta_{k}=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}
. For any
f(x)\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and for any
0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{4n}
, let
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n+1
.
Define the threshold
R_{k}:=\frac{16\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n\right)
, where
\Delta_{1}
is defined in (
B.1
).
For any index
l\ \in[d]
, if
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\geq R_{k}
, then with probability at least

1-n\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})(\frac{28}{3n}+\frac{8}{3}\delta)}\right),

we have the following concentration bound:

\frac{C_{\text{lower}}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\leq\frac{C_{\text{upper}}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}},

(4.1)

where the constants are defined as:

C_{\text{lower}}:=1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{4n}{(1-2n\delta)\beta_{2}^{n}},\quad C_{\text{upper}}:=\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{8n}{(1-2n\delta)\beta_{2}^{n}}\right)^{-1/2}.

The proof of Lemma
4.1
is shown in Section
5
. By Lemma
4.1
, we have
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}
as
\beta_{2}\rightarrow 1
.

Step II: Potential function to handle
m_{k}
.

Note that the concentration effect of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}
alone is not enough to establish the convergence of Adam. This is because
m_{k}
also contains heavy historical gradient signals which further distort the update direction from the gradient direction. In other words, it is still unclear whether
\frac{\mathbb{E}_{k}(m_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}
is close to
\nabla f(x_{k})
.

We use a potential function
f(z_{k})
to offset the effect of
m_{k}
, where
f(\cdot)
is the original function in the problem (
1.1
) and
z_{k}:=\frac{x_{k}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k-n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}
. The auxiliary sequence
z_{k}
can help cancel out all historical signals up to
(k-n)
-th iteration, which provides more convenience to the convergence analysis.

\displaystyle z_{k}-z_{k+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{x_{k}-x_{k+1}-\beta_{1}^{n}\left(x_{k-n}-x_{k+1-n}\right)}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}=\frac{1}{{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}}\cdot\left(\eta_{k}\frac{m_{k}}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\frac{m_{k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{k-n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_concentrate_v}}}{\approx}

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k}}{{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}}\cdot\frac{\nabla f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})+\beta_{1}\nabla f_{\tau_{k-1}}(x_{k-1})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-1}\nabla f_{\tau_{k-n}}(x_{k-n})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{k})}}

Note that the potential function is inspired by the following
L_{k}
from SGD analysis
(
58
)
:

\vskip-2.84526ptL_{k}=\left(f\left(\widetilde{z}_{k}\right)-f^{\star}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}c_{i}\left\|x_{k+1-i}-x_{k-i}\right\|^{2},\quad\widetilde{z}_{k}=\frac{x_{k}-\beta_{1}x_{k-1}}{1-\beta_{1}},\quad c_{i}>0\vskip-2.84526pt

We find that this idea of constructing
L^{k}
and
\widetilde{z}_{k}
is useful for Adam analysis, and we make the following changes.

•

Change I:
For Adam, we find that
L_{k}
can be simplified by setting
c_{i}=0
. This is because the purpose of introducing
c_{i}
is to help bound the update magnitude
\frac{m_{k}}{\sqrt{v_{k}}}
, which is inherently bounded for Adam (shown later in Lemma
B.2
in Appendix
6
).

•

Change II:
We replace
\widetilde{z}_{k}
with
z_{k}
, which differs by retaining
(n-1)
additional historical gradient signals. This modification enlarges the convergence region in the
(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
diagram: with
\widetilde{z}_{k}
, convergence requires

\beta_{2}\geq 1-\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{n^{5}}\right),

whereas with
z_{k}
, the requirement is relaxed to

\beta_{2}\geq 1-\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}{n^{5}}\right),

which defines a strictly larger region.

With the help of Lemma
4.1
and the potential function
f(z_{k})
, we show that Adam’s update direction is close to
\nabla f(x_{k})
when
\beta_{2}
is large enough, which can lead to convergence. We find that it is rather non-trivial to apply the concentration results in Lemma
4.1
to the convergence proof due to the existence of momentum, possibly unbounded gradients, and multiple coupling effects. We propose a systematic procedure to overcome them in Lemma
4.2
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Consider the same condition as Lemma
4.1
;
when
k\in\mathbb{N}
satisfies
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log\frac{1}{4}}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+2n+1
and

\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq\frac{1}{4nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}
and
\beta_{1}^{k-n}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
, we have

\vskip-5.69054pt\mathbb{E}\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{nd\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2nd^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{k}},

where constant
C>0
and
\delta(\beta_{2})
is a constant that approaches 0 as
\beta_{2}
approaches to 1 (see (
6.46
) in App.
6
).
The proof of Lemma
4.2
is in Section
6
and the whole proof of Theorem
3.1
is in Section
7
.

5
Proof of Lemma
4.1

The proof proceeds in three steps. In Step I, we derive deterministic algebraic bounds relating
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}
to
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}
via the ratio
\frac{\max_{i}(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}
. In Step II, we establish a lower bound on
\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})
using the geometric-sum structure of Adam, which yields an upper bound on this ratio. In Step III, we apply Bernstein’s inequality to show that the lower bound on the geometric sum holds with high probability, thereby establishing the concentration result.

Step I: Deterministic algebraic bounds relating
1/\sqrt{v_{l,k}}
to
1/\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}
.

We begin by establishing the relationship between
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}
.

For the lower bound, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)+\left(v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)\right)}}

(5.1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{\left|v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)\right|}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\left(1-\frac{\left|v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)\right|}{2\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\right),

where the last inequality uses
\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+x}}\geq 1-\frac{x}{2}
for
x\geq 0
.

To proceed, we bound
\left|v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)\right|
. By the update rule of
v_{l,k}
, we have

\left|v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)\right|=(1-\beta_{2})\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})\right)^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})\right)^{2}\right]\right|\leq 2(1-\beta_{2})\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}.

(5.2)

Substituting (
5.2
) into (
5.1
), we obtain

\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\right).

(5.3)

For the upper bound, we proceed similarly:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)+\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}

(5.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}},

To obtain a meaningful upper bound from (
5.4
), we need to ensure that the denominator remains positive and bounded away from zero. In particular, we want to have

\left|\frac{v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\right|<1.

(5.5)

By (
5.2
), condition (
5.5
) holds if the following holds

2(1-\beta_{2})\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}<1.

(5.6)

Assuming (
5.6
) holds, we have
1+\frac{v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\geq 1-\left|\frac{v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\right|>0
. This allows us to bound the reciprocal square root:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\left|\frac{v_{l,k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\right|}}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq_vlk_deviation}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2(1-\beta_{2})\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}}.

Combining (
5.4
) and (
5.7
), we obtain the following (assuming (
5.6
) holds):

\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2(1-\beta_{2})\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}}.

(5.8)

The bounds (
5.3
) and (
5.8
) are useful only if we can bound the ratio
\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}
and verify condition (
5.6
). A trivial bound follows immediately:

\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\leq\frac{n\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})\right)^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\leq\frac{n\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})\right)^{2}\right]}{(1-\beta_{2})\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})\right)^{2}\right]}=\frac{n}{1-\beta_{2}},

(5.9)

which diverges as
\beta_{2}\to 1^{-}
. Substituting this into (
5.6
) would require
(1-\beta_{2})\cdot\frac{2n}{1-\beta_{2}}=2n<1
, which fails for any
n\geq 1
. Thus, the trivial bound is insufficient.

Our goal is to derive a refined bound such that

(1-\beta_{2})\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }\beta_{2}\to 1^{-},

(5.10)

which ensures that condition (
5.6
) is satisfied for
\beta_{2}
sufficiently close to 1, and that both (
5.3
) and (
5.8
) remain tight. Establishing such a bound requires substantial effort, which we undertake in the following Steps II and III.

Step II: Lower bounding
\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})
via the geometric-sum structure.

Define
\alpha:=\arg\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|
. Expanding
\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k}}(x_{k})\right)^{2}\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\beta_{2}^{j}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})\right)^{2}\right)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\beta_{2}^{j}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\beta_{2}^{j}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k-j})\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\tau_{k-j}=\alpha\right).

Notice that when
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n+1
, the condition of Lemma
4.1
implies the condition of Lemma
B.3
for
j\leq\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n
. Applying Lemma
B.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{2}(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n}\beta_{2}^{j}\mathbb{I}\left(\tau_{k-j}=\alpha\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{2}(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil}\beta_{2}^{t}\mathbb{I}\left(\tau_{k-n-t}=\alpha\right).

For
k^{*}<k-n
, define the auxiliary quantity

Y_{k^{*},k}(i):=(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\beta_{2}^{j}\mathbb{I}\left(\tau_{k-n-j}=i\right),\quad\forall\,i\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\},

(5.12)

which represents a weighted count of how many times index
i
is sampled within the
k^{*}
-step historical window preceding step
k-n
. Since
\tau_{k-n-j}
is sampled uniformly from
\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
, the sequence
\{\mathbb{I}(\tau_{k-n-j}=i)\}_{j=1}^{k^{*}}
forms a sequence of i.i.d. Bernoulli random variables with success probability
\frac{1}{n}
.

Step III: Concentration of
Y_{k^{*},k}(i)
via Bernstein’s inequality and the choice of
k^{*}
.

Our objective is to establish that for any
0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{4n}
, the random variable
Y_{k^{*},k}(\alpha)
satisfies
Y_{k^{*},k}(\alpha)\geq\frac{1}{2n}-\delta
with probability approaching 1 as
\beta_{2}\to 1^{-}
, where
\alpha:=\arg\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|
. The concentration analysis is enabled by the geometric weighting structure: each term in
Y_{k^{*},k}(\alpha)
is scaled by the coefficient
(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{j}
, whose magnitude is controlled by
(1-\beta_{2})
. As
\beta_{2}\to 1^{-}
, this coefficient vanishes, allowing us to apply Bernstein’s inequality to get a sharp probability bound. However, direct analysis is complicated because the index
\alpha=\arg\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|
depends on the random iterate
x_{k}
. Consequently,
\alpha
is a random variable coupled with the optimization trajectory, making the distribution of
\mathbb{I}\left(\tau_{k-n-j}=\alpha\right)
difficult to characterize. To circumvent this, we first establish concentration for an arbitrary fixed index
i\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
, then apply a union bound.

Define the events

\mathcal{E}_{k}(i):=\left\{Y_{k^{*},k}(i)\geq\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right\},\quad\forall\,i\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\},

(5.13)

and the global failure event

\mathcal{U}_{k}:=\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}\left\{Y_{k^{*},k}(i)<\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right\}=\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathcal{E}_{k}(i)^{c}.

(5.14)

Our goal is to bound the probability
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{k}(\alpha)^{c})
. Since
\alpha
takes some value in
[n]
, we observe that the failure event for
\alpha
is contained within the global failure event:

\mathcal{E}_{k}(\alpha)^{c}=\left\{Y_{k^{*},k}(\alpha)<\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right\}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{k}.

By the union bound,

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{k}(\alpha)^{c}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}_{k}\right)\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{P}\left(Y_{k^{*},k}(i)<\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right)\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{P}\left(Y_{k^{*},k}(i)\leq\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right).

(5.15)

It thus suffices to bound
\mathbb{P}\left(Y_{k^{*},k}(i)\leq\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right)
for an arbitrary fixed
i
.

For ease of presentation, we simplify notation by fixing an arbitrary index
i\in[n]
, drop the variable
k
and replace the subscript
\tau_{k-n-j}
with
\tau_{j}
and redefine

Y_{k^{*}}:=(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\beta_{2}^{j}\mathbb{I}(\tau_{j}=i).

To apply Bernstein’s inequality, we introduce centered random variables. Define

X_{j}:=(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{j}\left[\mathbb{I}(\tau_{j}=i)-\frac{1}{n}\right]

for
j=1,\ldots,k^{*}
. Then
\mathbb{E}[X_{j}]=0
and
|X_{j}|\leq(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{j}
for all
j
. We can express

Y_{k^{*}}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{k^{*}}\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{k^{*}}X_{j}.

Before applying Bernstein’s inequality, we first lower bound the expectation of
Y_{k^{*}}
and upper bound its variance. When
\beta_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
and
k^{*}
is sufficiently large such that
\beta_{2}^{k^{*}}\leq n\delta
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{k^{*}}\right]=(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\beta_{2}^{j}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbb{I}(\tau_{j}=i)\bigr]=\frac{\beta_{2}(1-\beta_{2}^{k^{*}})}{n}\geq\frac{1}{2n}-\frac{\delta}{2}.

(5.16)

For the variance, since the
X_{j}
are independent, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}(Y_{k^{*}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\operatorname{Var}(X_{j})=(1-\beta_{2})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\beta_{2}^{2j}\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\mathbb{I}(\tau_{j}=i)\bigr)

(5.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\beta_{2})^{2}\beta_{2}^{2}}{1-\beta_{2}^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)\leq\frac{(1-\beta_{2})^{2}}{n(1-\beta_{2}^{2})}\leq\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{n}.

We now apply Bernstein’s inequality. Note that for all
j\in\{1,\dots,k^{*}\}
, the random variables
X_{j}
satisfy the uniform bound
|X_{j}|\leq(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{j}\leq 1-\beta_{2}
. Applying Bernstein’s inequality and using the variance bound (
5.17
), we obtain for any
a>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Y_{k^{*}}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{k^{*}}\right]\leq-a\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}/2}{\operatorname{Var}(Y_{k^{*}})+\frac{1}{3}(1-\beta_{2})a}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}/2}{\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{3}(1-\beta_{2})a}\right).

Let
a=\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{k^{*}}\right]-(\frac{1}{2n}-\delta)
and notice that
a\leq\frac{1}{2n}+\delta
. When
a\geq\frac{\delta}{2}
, which holds for
\beta_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
and
k^{*}\geq\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Y_{k^{*}}\leq\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}/2}{\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{3}(1-\beta_{2})a}\right)

(5.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{(\delta/2)^{2}/2}{\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{3}(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{1}{2n}+\delta\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{28}{3n}+\frac{8}{3}\delta\right)}\right).

This concentration bound holds for any fixed index
i\in[n]
. Substituting (
5.18
) into the union bound (
5.15
), we obtain

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{k}(\alpha)^{c})\leq n\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{28}{3n}+\frac{8}{3}\delta\right)}\right).

We obtain that with probability at least
1-n\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{28}{3n}+\frac{8}{3}\delta\right)}\right)
, the inequality
Y_{k^{*},k}(\alpha)\geq\frac{1}{2n}-\delta
holds.

Setting
k^{*}=\lceil\frac{\log(n\delta)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil
and substituting this result into the lower bound for
\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)
(
5
), we conclude that with probability at least
1-n\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{28}{3n}+\frac{8}{3}\delta\right)}\right)
,

\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)\geq\frac{(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k}))^{2}}{2}\cdot\beta_{2}^{n}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2n}-\delta\right).

(5.19)

Recalling that
0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{4n}
, we equivalently obtain

\frac{\max_{i}\bigl(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\bigr)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\equiv\frac{(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k}))^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\leq\frac{4n}{(1-2n\delta)\beta_{2}^{n}}.

(5.20)

Finally, we verify that condition (
5.6
) is satisfied for
\beta_{2}
sufficiently close to 1. Substituting the bound (
5.20
) into (
5.6
), we require

(1-\beta_{2})2\frac{\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}\leq(1-\beta_{2})\frac{8n}{(1-2n\delta)\beta_{2}^{n}}<1.

(5.21)

Rearranging (
5.21
), we obtain the sufficient condition

\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{\beta_{2}^{n}}<\frac{1-2n\delta}{8n}=\frac{1}{8n}-\frac{\delta}{4}.

(5.22)

Since
0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{4n}
, the right-hand side of (
5.22
) is at least
\frac{1}{16n}>0
. The left-hand side
\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{\beta_{2}^{n}}
is strictly decreasing in
\beta_{2}
and vanishes as
\beta_{2}\to 1^{-}
. Therefore, (
5.22
) holds when
\beta_{2}
sufficiently close to 1 (e.g., when we choose
1-\beta_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1/n^{1.5})
), validating condition (
5.6
) and the upper bound (
5.8
).

Substituting (
5.20
) into the concentration bounds, we have with probability at least
1-n\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{28}{3n}+\frac{8}{3}\delta\right)}\right)
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{4n}{(1-2n\delta)\beta_{2}^{n}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{8n}{(1-2n\delta)\beta_{2}^{n}}}}\right).

Since the choice of
l
was arbitrary, this bound holds for all
l\in[d]
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
4.1
. ∎

6
Proof of Lemma
4.2

To prove Lemma
4.2
, we need to prove that the update direction of Adam lies in the dual cone of the negative gradient direction, i.e., it is a descent direction. Recall that

z_{k}:=\frac{x_{k}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k-n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}.

We aim to establish a lower bound on
\mathbb{E}\left\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\right\rangle
, which we decompose as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left\langle\nabla f(z_{k})-\nabla f(x_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\right\rangle}_{:=(a)}+\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left\langle\nabla f(x_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\right\rangle}_{:=(b)}.

Now we bound both terms.
\mathbb{E}(a)
can be simply bounded by using Lemma
B.2
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Cauchy-Schwarz}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\|\nabla f(z_{k})-\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}\|x_{k}-x_{k+1}-\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k-n}-x_{k-n+1})\|_{2}

(6.1)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Assumption }\ref{assum1}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{L}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left\|\frac{x_{k}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k-n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}-x_{k}\right\|_{2}\left(\|x_{k}-x_{k+1}\|_{2}+\beta_{1}^{n}\|x_{k-n}-x_{k-n+1}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{L\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(\left\|x_{k}-x_{k-1}\right\|_{2}+\cdots+\left\|x_{k-n+1}-x_{k-n}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(\|x_{k}-x_{k+1}\|_{2}+\beta_{1}^{n}\|x_{k-n}-x_{k-n+1}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\eqref{eq:m_over_v}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{L\beta_{1}^{n}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\left(\frac{d(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)^{2}\left(\eta_{k-1}+\eta_{k-2}+\cdots+\eta_{k-n}\right)\left(\eta_{k}+\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{k\geq n+1}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{L\beta_{1}^{n}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\left(\frac{d(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)^{2}n\sqrt{n+1}\eta_{k}\left(\eta_{k}+\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\eta_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{L\eta_{0}\beta_{1}^{n}d^{2}(1-\beta_{1})^{2}(1+\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1})n\sqrt{n+1}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}(1-\beta_{2})(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}})^{2}}:=-\eta_{k}\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}.

To handle term
(b)
, we first divide the analysis into two cases for each dimension
l
:

•

Case 1:
the
bounded
partial gradient event
B_{l,k}
, defined as
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\leq Q_{k}
; The definition of constant
Q_{k}
can be seen later in (
B.1
).

•

Case 2:
the complement of Case 1, the
unbounded
partial gradient event
B_{l,k}^{c}
.

Note that the former case is easier due to the bounded gradient condition. The latter case is more involved, and we will handle it using Lemma
4.1
. We first state two observations, which would help the subsequent analysis.

•

Observation 1:
When applied with
\delta=\frac{1}{4n}
, Lemma
4.1
guarantees that the following two key inequalities ((
6.2
) and (
6.3
)) hold with high probability for integer
\bar{k}\in[k-n,k]
, provided that the condition
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{\bar{k}})|\geq R_{\bar{k}}
is met:

\displaystyle\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{k}}(v_{l,\bar{k}})}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,\bar{k}}}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{16n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{k}}(v_{l,\bar{k}})}}.

(6.2)

\displaystyle\frac{\max_{i}(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{\bar{k}}))^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{\bar{k}}(v_{l,\bar{k}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4n}{(1-2n\delta)\beta_{2}^{n}}=\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}},

(6.3)

•

Observation 2:
When
B_{l,k}^{c}
occurs, (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) hold for all
\bar{k}\in[k-n,k]
with a probability of at least
1-n(n+1)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{160(1-\beta_{2})n}\right)
.

Observation 1 can be seen directly from the statement of Lemma
4.1
and its proof (
5.20
). Observation 2 can be shown as follows:

First, by Lemma
B.2
and the definition of
Q_{k}
, one can easily see that: under event
B_{l,k}^{c}
, the conditions of Lemma
4.1
are automatically satisfied
for all
integers
\bar{k}\in[k-n,k]
.

Second, under the conditions of Lemma
4.1
, the event of “inequalities (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) are satisfied for a given
\bar{k}
” is equivalent to the
event
\mathcal{E}_{\bar{k}}(\alpha(\bar{k})):=\{Y_{k^{*},\bar{k}}(\alpha(\bar{k}))\geq\frac{1}{4n}\}
. This can be easily seen from the proof of Lemma
4.1
. As such, to prove observation 2, one need to calculate the probability of event
\bigcap_{\bar{k}=k-n}^{k}\mathcal{E}_{\bar{k}}(\alpha(\bar{k}))
.

Third, instead of directly working on
\bigcap_{\bar{k}=k-n}^{k}\mathcal{E}_{\bar{k}}(\alpha(\bar{k}))
, we consider a slightly more restricted event:

A_{k}:=\bigcap_{i=0}^{n-1}\bigcap_{\bar{k}=k-n}^{k}\left\{Y_{k^{*},\bar{k}}(i)\geq\tfrac{1}{4n}\right\}.

(6.4)

The definition of
A_{k}
avoids events’ dependency on
\alpha(\bar{k})
, which will benefit the subsequent analysis. The probability of the tail event
A_{k}^{c}
can be easily calculated by applying the union bound and (
5.18
), and one can get
\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})\leq n(n+1)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{160(1-\beta_{2})n}\right)
.

Finally, event
A_{k}
implies event
\bigcap_{\bar{k}=k-n}^{k}\mathcal{E}_{\bar{k}}(\alpha(\bar{k}))
, which is further equivalent to (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) under
B_{l,k}^{c}
. Therefore, under
B_{l,k}^{c}
, (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) hold for all
\bar{k}\in[k-n,k]
with a probability of at least
1-n(n+1)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{160(1-\beta_{2})n}\right)
. This concludes the proof for observation 2.

Our subsequent analysis is centered on Observations 1 and 2. We first decompose the summation in term
(b)
into three distinct parts to facilitate the analysis of its expectation:

\displaystyle(b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\eta_{k}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right]

(6.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\left(\eta_{k}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(B_{l,k})\right]}_{(c)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\left(\eta_{k}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]}_{(d)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\left(\eta_{k}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]}_{(e)},

where
B_{l,k},A_{k}
and their complements
B_{l,k}^{c},A_{k}^{c}
are defined in the previous paragraphs and (
6.4
).

Road map for the rest of the proof.

We will provide a lower bound for each term. We will show that Term
(d)
constitutes the descent direction, and
(c)
&
(e)
are either smaller than
(d)
or vanishing with iteration
k
.
Among the three terms, term
(c)
and
(e)
are easier to handle: term
(c)
addresses the bounded gradient scenario, and term
(e)
accounts for the tail event
A_{k}^{c}
. The term
(d)
will be more involved, and it will be further divided into more sub-terms and we will resort to Lemma
4.1
.

Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(c)
.

We start with
\mathbb{E}(c)
. Based on the proof of Lemma
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(c)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\eta_{k}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{|m_{l,k}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}+\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{|m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(\text{$B_{l,k}$})\right]

(6.6)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:m_over_v}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\eta_{k}Q_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}+\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}Q_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(\text{$B_{l,k}$})\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}d\eta_{k}Q_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\left(1+\beta_{1}^{n}\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{k-n}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{k\geq n+1}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}dQ_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\left(1+\sqrt{n+1}\beta_{1}^{n}\right):=-\eta_{k}\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{k}}.

Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(e)
.

Now we derive a lower bound for the term
\mathbb{E}(e)
. By applying (
B.5
) and using
\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\leq\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\eta_{k}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]

(6.7)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:m_over_v}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\eta_{k}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}+\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{k\geq n+1}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(1+\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(1+\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right].

Here, the penultimate inequality holds because
k\geq n+1
, which ensures
\frac{\eta_{k-n}}{\eta_{k}}=\sqrt{\frac{k}{k-n}}\leq\sqrt{n+1}
.

To upper bound
\mathbb{E}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})
, we observe that the event
A_{k}^{c}
depends on the sampled indices within a historical window
\{\tau_{j}\}_{j=k-2n-k^{*}}^{k-n-1}
. These indices are correlated with
x_{k}
through the algorithm’s update recursion, thereby inducing a dependence between
|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|
and
\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})
. To get a desired upper bound, we perform the following decoupling procedures. First, we introduce a lag parameter

h:=2n+k^{*},\qquad\text{where}\quad k^{*}=\left\lceil\frac{\log(1/4)}{\log\beta_{2}}\right\rceil.

(6.8)

We now shift from
x_{k}
to the earlier iterate
x_{k-h}
. By the condition of Lemma
4.2
,
k-h
is greater than 0, and thus it is a valid index. Further, the event
A_{k}^{c}
depends on the indices

\{\tau_{j}\}_{j=k-2n-k^{*}}^{k-n-1}=\{\tau_{j}\}_{j=k-h}^{k-n-1},

which are sampled independently after
x_{k-h}
and thus are independent of
\mathcal{F}_{k-h}
. Consequently,
x_{k-h}
and
\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})
are independent random variables, yielding

\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{l}f(x_{k-h})|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{l}f(x_{k-h})|\right]\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c}).

(6.9)

Using Lemma
B.2
to relate gradients at
x_{k}
and
x_{k-h}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(|\partial_{l}f(x_{k-h})|+h\Delta_{k-h}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right]

(6.10)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:independence_e}}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(|\partial_{l}f(x_{k-h})|+h\Delta_{k-h}\right)\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|+2dh\,\Delta_{k-h}\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c}),

where in the last inequality we have applied Lemma
B.2
once again to convert

\mathbb{E}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k-h})|
back to the current iterate
x_{k}
, incurring an additive error of magnitude
h\Delta_{k-h}
.

Combining (
6.7
) and (
6.10
), and using

\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\leq d\,|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|
, where
\partial_{\alpha}f(x):=\max_{l}|\partial_{l}f(x)|
, together with

\Delta_{k-h}\leq\sqrt{h+1}\Delta_{1}/\sqrt{k}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(e)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(1+\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\right)\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\mathbb{E}\left[d|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|+2dh\,\Delta_{k-h}\right]

(6.11)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\,\delta_{1}(\beta_{2})\,\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\right]-\eta_{k}\frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{k}},

where we define

\delta_{1}(\beta_{2}):=\frac{d\left(1+\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\right)}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}},\quad C_{3}:=\frac{2\,dh\sqrt{h+1}\left(1+\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\right)}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\Delta_{1}.

(6.12)

Recall from the union bound and (
5.18
) that

\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})\leq n(n+1)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{160(1-\beta_{2})n}\right).

As
\beta_{2}\to 1^{-}
, the probability
\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})
decays exponentially at rate
O\left(\exp\left(-\frac{1}{160(1-\beta_{2})n}\right)\right)
, which dominates the polynomial growth of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}
. Consequently, the factor

\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}
vanishes as
\beta_{2}\to 1^{-}
, ensuring that
\delta_{1}(\beta_{2})\to 0
as
\beta_{2}\to 1^{-}
.
This concludes the lower bound for
\mathbb{E}(e)
.

Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(d)
.

The lower bound of
(d)
is more involved due to the existence of momentum and unbounded gradients. We first convert all
v_{l,k-n}
’s to
v_{l,k}
’s. We discuss two cases.

Case (1): when
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})m_{l,k-n}\leq 0
.

Recall
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,\bar{k}-n}}}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,\bar{k}}}}\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}

, we have

-\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\geq-\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}.

Case (2): when
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})m_{l,k-n}\geq 0
.

In this case, we first provide a lower bound on
v_{l,\bar{k}-1}
for the integer
\bar{k}\in[k-n+1,k]
:

\displaystyle v_{l,\bar{k}-1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle v_{l,\bar{k}}\left(1-\frac{|v_{l,\bar{k}-1}-v_{l,\bar{k}}|}{v_{l,\bar{k}}}\right)

(6.13)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\geq}

\displaystyle v_{l,\bar{k}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{\bar{k}})|^{2}+v_{l,\bar{k}-1}}{v_{l,\bar{k}}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq_concentrate_v_epsilon_given})(\ref{upperbound_of_f_over_v_epsilon})}{\geq}

\displaystyle v_{l,\bar{k}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right).

Then we have

-\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\overset{(\ref{eq:v_k-1_geq_v_k})}{\geq}-\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{n}{2}}.

Combining the two cases, we have

\displaystyle-\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{|m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}},

(6.14)

where

\delta_{2}(\beta_{2}):=\left|\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}-1\right|+\left|\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{n}{2}}-1\right|.

(6.15)

We briefly comment on the order of
\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})
w.r.t.
n
. Assume we are using
1-\beta_{2}=\mathcal{O}(n^{-a})
with
a>0
, then by basic calculation, one can show that the 1st term is
\mathcal{O}(n^{-(a-1)})
and the 2nd term is
\mathcal{O}(n^{-(a-2)})
.

With the inequality (
6.14
) above, we have converted all
v_{l,k-n}
’s in term
(d)
to
v_{l,k}
’s. Now we further decompose
(d)
into three terms
(d_{1})
,
(d_{2})
and
(d_{3})
:

\displaystyle(d)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\left(\eta_{k}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\frac{m_{l,k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{|m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]

(6.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\left(\eta_{k}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}(m_{l,k}-\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{k-n}}\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-n})-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{|m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\left(\eta_{k}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\left((m_{l,k}-\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-n})+(1-\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{k-n}})\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-n})\right)-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-n}\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\frac{|m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})+\underbrace{\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}}_{:=F_{k}}\underbrace{\left(\beta_{1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1}}(x_{k-1})+\dots+\beta_{1}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-n+1}}(x_{k-n+1})\right)}_{:=G}\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\left(\eta_{k}\frac{n+1}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}+\eta_{k}\frac{\sqrt{n+1}\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})\right)\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})||m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})}_{:=(d_{3})}

\displaystyle\overset{}{=}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})}_{:=(d_{1})}+\underbrace{\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}F_{k}\cdot G\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})}_{:=(d_{2})}+(d_{3}).

where (i) is due to
|1-\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{k-n}}|\leq\frac{n+1}{\sqrt{k}}
.

In the following, we will show that
(d_{1})
constitutes the descent direction, whereas
(d_{2})
and
(d_{3})
are error terms.

Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(d_{1})
.

We first bound
\mathbb{E}(d_{1})
. We first derive a lower bound for the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}(d_{1})
and subsequently take the outer expectation. By Lemma
4.1
, we control the quantity
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}
via
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}
, which yields:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k}(d_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right)

(6.17)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\left[\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right)-\delta_{3}(\beta_{2})\left|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})\right|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\left[\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\right)-\delta_{3}(\beta_{2})\left|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\left[\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k})\right)-\delta_{3}(\beta_{2})\left|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})\right|-\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{2\sqrt{2n}d}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}Q_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(iii)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(v_{l,k}\right)}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})-\delta_{3}(\beta_{2})\left|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})\right|-\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{2\sqrt{2n}d}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}Q_{k}\right],

where
\alpha:=\arg\max_{l}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|
and
v_{\alpha,k}:=\max_{l}v_{l,k}
. The steps (i), (ii), (iii) are justified as follows:

Justification for (i) in (
6.17
):

We apply the concentration inequality (
6.2
) from Lemma
4.1
to split the term into two parts based on the sign of
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})
:

•

When
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})\geq 0
, we use the lower bound in (
6.2
):

\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\geq\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}.

•

When
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})<0
, we use the upper bound in (
6.2
):

\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{16n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}.

Combining these two cases and collecting error terms, we obtain the coefficient
\delta_{3}(\beta_{2})
defined as:

\delta_{3}(\beta_{2})=\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(\frac{(1-\beta_{2})16\sqrt{2n}nd}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{3n}}}+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-(1-\beta_{2})\frac{16n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}}-1\right)\frac{2\sqrt{2n}d}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right).

The proof follows a similar strategy as in (
6.14
), where we decomposed the term based on the sign of the product.

Justification for (ii) in (
6.17
):

Under the event
B_{l,k}
, we have
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\leq\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\leq Q_{k}
. Additionally, under the event
A_{k}
, Lemma
4.1
(specifically inequality (
6.3
)) ensures that:

\frac{\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}.

Therefore, under
A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}
, we have:

\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\right|\leq Q_{k}\cdot\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}=\frac{2\sqrt{2n}Q_{k}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}.

Justification for (iii) in (
6.17
):

This step exploits two key facts:

(i)

Fact 1:
By the inequality
\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\geq\frac{1}{n}
, we can lower bound the coefficient.

(ii)

Fact 2:
The event
A_{k}
depends only on the history up to step
k-n
, and hence is independent of the current gradient sample
\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})
conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{k}
. Therefore:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{k}\left(\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\partial_{l}f_{{\tau_{k}}}(x_{k})\right)}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k})

(6.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\left(1-\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c}).

This completes the derivation of the lower bound for
\mathbb{E}_{k}(d_{1})
in (
6.17
).

Now we proceed to handle (
6.17
). Taking the expectation over (
6.17
), we now upper bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right]
. We will use the upper bound (
5.9
) and the same decoupling strategy in (
6.11
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\right)}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{l,k})}}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:trivial}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})}}|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})dh}{\sqrt{n(1-\beta_{2})}}\mathbb{E}|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|+\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})2dh}{\sqrt{n(1-\beta_{2})}}\sqrt{h+1}\frac{\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}.

Combining the results above, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(d_{1})=\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}_{k}(d_{1})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\left[\mathbb{E}\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})\mathbb{E}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})\right|-\frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{k}}\right],

(6.19)

where

\displaystyle\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})dh}{\sqrt{n(1-\beta_{2})}}+\delta_{3}(\beta_{2}),

(6.20)

\displaystyle\quad C_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{c})2dh}{\sqrt{n(1-\beta_{2})}}\sqrt{h+1}\Delta_{1}+\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{2\sqrt{2n}d}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}Q_{1}.

(6.21)

This concludes the lower bound for
\mathbb{E}(d_{1})
in (
6.16
).

Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(d_{2})
.

To bound the term
\mathbb{E}(d_{2})
in (
6.16
), we convert all the variables
x
’s to
x_{k-n}
and then take the expectation conditioned on the history up to
\mathcal{F}_{k-n}
.
We denote our target terms as

F_{k-n}:=\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-n})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\quad\text{and}\quad G_{k-n}:=\beta_{1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1}}(x_{k-n})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-n+1}}(x_{k-n}).

(6.22)

We now convert
F_{k}
in (
6.16
) to
F_{k-n}
using the following Lemma
6.1
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Consider Algorithm
1
. Assume that
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/4)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+2n+1
, and for all integers
\bar{k}\in[k-n,k]
,
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{\bar{k}})|\geq R_{\bar{k}}
, and the joint event
A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c}
occurs.
Then, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-n})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right|\leq\frac{n}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\frac{\Delta_{k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}+n\delta_{5}(\beta_{2}),

(6.23)

where
\Delta_{k-n}
is defined as in Lemma
B.2
, and
\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})
is defined as follows.

\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})=\delta_{5,1}(\beta_{2})\delta_{5,2}(\beta_{2})\delta_{5,3}(\beta_{2}),

(6.24)

with
\delta_{5,1}(\beta_{2})=\left(\left[1-\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}-1\right)
,

\delta_{5,2}(\beta_{2})=\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and

\delta_{5,3}(\beta_{2})=\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}
.

The proof of Lemma
6.1
can be seen in Appendix
C
.

With Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
B.2
, we have the following results (
G,F_{k-n},G_{k-n}
are defined in (
6.16
) and (
6.22
)).

|F_{k}-F_{k-n}|\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma_k-k-n_WR}}{\leq}\frac{n}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\frac{\Delta_{k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}+n\delta_{5}(\beta_{2}):=C_{F,k},

|G_{k-n}-G|\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}\sum_{m=1}^{n-1}\beta_{1}^{m}\left(\sum_{\bar{k}=m}^{n-1}\Delta_{k-\bar{k}}\right)\leq n^{2}\Delta_{k-n}\leq n^{2}\Delta_{k}\sqrt{n+1}:=C_{G,k}.

Then,

\displaystyle(d_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}(F_{k}\cdot G)\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

(6.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}F_{k}\cdot[G_{k-n}-(G_{k-n}-G)]\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left[F_{k}\cdot G_{k-n}-|F_{k}|\cdot|G_{k-n}-G|\right]\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left[F_{k-n}\cdot G_{k-n}-|F_{k-n}-F_{k}|\cdot|G_{k-n}|-|F_{k}|\cdot|G_{k-n}-G|\right]\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sum_{l=1}^{d}\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-n})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\left(\beta_{1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1}}(x_{k-n})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-n+1}}(x_{k-n})\right)\right]\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})}_{:=(d_{2,1})}

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{d}\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\bigg[\left|C_{F,k}\cdot G_{k-n}\right|+\left|F_{k}\cdot C_{G,k}\right|\bigg]\cdot\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c}).

We can bound
\mathbb{E}(d_{2,1})
by following the same procedure used to derive the lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(d_{1})
in (
6.17
) and (
6.19
).
We first note the following fact (similar to the fact (
6.18
)): Since event
A_{k}
depends only on the history up to the
(k-n)
-th iteration and
\tau_{\bar{k}}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{k-n}
for each
\bar{k}\in[k-n,k]
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{k-n}\left[\partial_{l}f(x_{k-n})\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{\bar{k}}}(x_{k-n})\mathbb{I}(A_{k})\right]=\partial_{l}f(x_{k-n})\mathbb{E}_{k-n}[\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{\bar{k}}}(x_{k-n})]\mathbb{I}(A_{k})=(\partial_{l}f(x_{k-n}))^{2}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}).

(6.26)

Proceeding as before, we obtain the following bound using the fact that
\mathbb{I}(A_{k})=\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k})+\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(d_{2,1})=\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}_{k-n}(d_{2,1})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\beta_{1}^{i}\eta_{k}\left[\mathbb{E}\frac{\left(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k-n})\right)^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k-n}(v_{\alpha,k-n})}}-\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})\mathbb{E}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k-n})\right|-\frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{k-n}}\right]

(6.27)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\left[\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})\mathbb{E}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k-n})\right|+\frac{C_{4}\sqrt{n+1}}{\sqrt{k}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\left[\delta_{6}(\beta_{2})\mathbb{E}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|+\delta_{7}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}+\frac{C_{5}}{\sqrt{k}}\right],

where

\delta_{6}(\beta_{2})=\frac{\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})}{1-\beta_{1}}nd\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d,\quad\delta_{7}(\beta_{2})=\frac{\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})}{1-\beta_{1}}nd\sqrt{n}\sqrt{d},\quad C_{5}=\frac{\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})}{1-\beta_{1}}n^{2}d\Delta_{1}\sqrt{n+1}+\frac{C_{4}\sqrt{n+1}}{1-\beta_{1}}.

(6.28)

We deal with the rest of the terms in (

) similarly, using Lemma

, (

) and (

).

\displaystyle|C_{F,k}\cdot G_{k-n}|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{n}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\frac{\Delta_{k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}+n\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})\right)\left(\beta_{1}|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1}}(x_{k-n})|+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-1}|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-n+1}}(x_{k-n})|\right)\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

(6.29)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})}{1-\beta_{1}}\frac{\Delta_{1}2\sqrt{2n}n\sqrt{n+1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}+\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})\frac{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})}{1-\beta_{1}}n\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})\frac{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})}{1-\beta_{1}}n^{2}\sqrt{n+1}\Delta_{1}+\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})\frac{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})}{1-\beta_{1}}n\sqrt{n}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{D_{0}}.

And

\displaystyle|F_{k}\cdot C_{G,k}|\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}n^{2}\Delta_{k}\sqrt{n+1}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\overset{(\ref{eq_concentrate_v_epsilon_given})(\ref{upperbound_of_f_over_v_epsilon})}{\leq}\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}n^{2}\Delta_{k}\sqrt{n+1}.

(6.30)

Plug (
6.27
), (

), (
6.30
) into (
6.25

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(d_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:d2_unsimplified}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(d_{2,1})-\sum_{l=1}^{d}\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\bigg[\left|C_{F,k}\cdot G_{k-n}\right|+\left|F_{k}\cdot C_{G,k}\right|\bigg]\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:EEk-nd_2,1}(\ref{eq:C_F*Gbase})(\ref{eq:F_K*C_G})}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\left[\delta_{8}(\beta_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|\right]+\delta_{9}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}+\frac{C_{6}}{\sqrt{k}}\right],

where the constant terms are defined as follows (

,

and

are defined in (

(6.31)

(6.32)

C_{6}=C_{5}+d\frac{\Delta_{1}2\sqrt{2n}n\sqrt{n+1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}+d\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})\frac{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})}{1-\beta_{1}}n^{2}\sqrt{n+1}\Delta_{1}+\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}d\frac{\Delta_{1}2\sqrt{2n}n^{2}\sqrt{n+1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}.

(6.33)

Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(d_{3})
.

Now we provide a lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(d_{3})
in (

).
Since

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})||m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})\

\displaystyle-\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|m_{l,k-n}|.

(6.34)

We further bound

,

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}|m_{l,k-n}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{l=1}^{d}[|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-n}}(x_{k-n})|+\beta_{1}|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-n-1}}(x_{k-n-1})|+\cdots]+\beta_{1}^{k-n}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|m_{l,0}|

(6.35)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_fi_f}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{q=1}^{k-n}\beta_{1}^{q-1}\left[\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{2\sqrt{n+1}qd\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\right]+\beta_{1}^{k-n}\|m_{0}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_beta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\frac{2\sqrt{n+1}d\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}+\beta_{1}^{k-n}\|m_{0}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\frac{2\sqrt{n+1}d\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\|m_{0}\|_{1},

where (i) holds for
k
large enough such that
\beta_{1}^{k-n}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
.
Plug (
6.35
) into (
6.34
),

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})||m_{l,k-n}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\mathbb{I}(A_{k}\cap B_{l,k}^{c})

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:c_sum_m})}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|m_{l,k-n}|

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:sum_m})}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\left(\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{2\sqrt{n+1}d\Delta_{1}}{(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{k}}+\frac{\|m_{0}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\right).

Plug inequality (
6.36
) into the term
(d_{3})
in (

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(d_{3})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\left[\left(\delta_{10}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)\mathbb{E}|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|+\delta_{11}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}+\frac{C_{8}}{\sqrt{k}}\right],

(6.37)

where the constant terms are defined as follows (

is defined in (

).):

\displaystyle\delta_{10}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n+1}\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d,

(6.38)

\displaystyle\delta_{11}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n+1}\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\sqrt{n}\sqrt{d},

(6.39)

\displaystyle C_{7}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(n+1)\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d,

(6.40)

\displaystyle C_{8}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(n+1)\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\sqrt{n}\sqrt{D_{0}d}

(6.41)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{n+1}\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\delta_{2}(\beta_{2})\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}\left(\frac{2\sqrt{n+1}d\Delta_{1}}{(1-\beta_{1})}+\|m_{0}\|_{1}\right).

Summary of the analysis above.

Now we summarize the analysis above and provide a lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(b)\geq\mathbb{E}(c)+\mathbb{E}(d_{1})+\mathbb{E}(d_{2})+\mathbb{E}(d_{3})+\mathbb{E}(e)
in (
6.16
) by grouping the terms of
(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}
,
\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})
and gradient-independent errors.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(b)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\geq}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\mathbb{E}|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\cdot\left(\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})+\delta_{8}(\beta_{2})+\delta_{10}(\beta_{2})+\delta_{1}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)

(6.42)

\displaystyle-(\delta_{9}(\beta_{2})+\delta_{11}(\beta_{2}))\sqrt{D_{0}}-\frac{C_{2}+C_{3}+C_{4}+C_{6}+C_{8}}{\sqrt{k}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{}{:=}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\right]-\delta_{11}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}-\frac{C_{9}}{\sqrt{k}}\right\},

where

\displaystyle\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})+\delta_{8}(\beta_{2})+\delta_{10}(\beta_{2})+\delta_{1}(\beta_{2}),

\displaystyle C_{9}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2}+C_{3}+C_{4}+C_{6}+C_{8},

(6.43)

and
\delta_{4}(\beta_{2})
,
\delta_{8}(\beta_{2})
,
\delta_{10}(\beta_{2})
, and
\delta_{1}(\beta_{2})
are defined in (
6.20
), (
6.31
), (
6.38
), (
6.12
).
Note that
\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}
vanishes as
\beta_{2}\rightarrow 1
and
k\rightarrow\infty
.
C_{2},C_{3},C_{4},C_{6},C_{8}
are defined in (
6.6
), (
6.12
), (
6.21
), (
6.33
), (
6.41
), respectively.

Based on (
6.42
), one can show that the following (
6.44
) holds when
\beta_{2}
is large enough such that
\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})\leq\frac{1}{4nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}
and (iii)

is large enough such that
\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq\frac{1}{4nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}
.
Since
\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})
is a polynomial of

, condition (i) can be achieved by setting

.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\right]\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{nd\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2nd^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}-\frac{C_{11}}{\sqrt{k}},\

(6.44)

where
C_{11}=\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{(1-\beta_{2})D_{1}n^{3}d^{2}\sqrt{5}}+\frac{8\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+C_{7})}{(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{1}n}d}+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}
is defined later in (
6.48
).

The proof of (
6.44
) will be presented in the immediate future. Finally, we combine the lower bounds for term (a) (
6.1
) and term (b) (
6.42
) to get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle=\mathbb{E}[(a)+(b)]\overset{(\ref{eq:a})(\ref{eq:b})}{\geq}\eta_{k}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{nd\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2nd^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{k}}\right\},\

(6.45)

where

\delta(\beta_{2}):=\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})\frac{1}{\sqrt{D_{1}d}}+\delta_{11}(\beta_{2}),\quad C=C_{1}+C_{9}+C_{11},

(6.46)

and
\delta(\beta_{2})
approaches 0 as
\beta_{2}

\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})
and

are defined in (

) and (

and

are defined in (

) and (

), respectively;
C_{11}
is defined later in (

).
The proof of Lemma
4.2
is now complete.

Now we prove (
6.44
). We will derive a lower bound for
\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|
, where
\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})
and
C_{7}
are as defined earlier in (
6.43
). Note that
\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})
approaches 0 as
\beta_{2}
approaches 1, and
\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}
vanishes as
k
increases. We consider two cases:

Case (a): When
|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\geq\frac{4\sqrt{2}\Delta_{k}}{(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{1}n}d}
:

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(\partial_{\alpha}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\left(\partial_{\alpha}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})\right)^{2}\beta_{2}^{j}\right)}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\eqref{lemma_fi_f_2nd}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\left(\sqrt{\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\sqrt{D_{1}n}d\right)^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\left(\sqrt{\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\sqrt{D_{1}n}d+\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}\right)^{2}\beta_{2}^{j}\right)}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\beta_{2}^{j}\left(\left(\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}\right)D_{1}nd^{2}+4\sqrt{2}j\Delta_{k}\sqrt{\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\sqrt{D_{1}n}d+8j^{2}\Delta_{k}^{2}\right)\right)}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_beta}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{D_{1}nd^{2}\left(\left(\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}\right)+\sqrt{\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\frac{4\sqrt{2}\Delta_{k}}{(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{1}n}d}+\frac{16\Delta_{k}^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})^{2}D_{1}nd^{2}}\right)}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Case (a)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}D_{1}nd^{2}\left(\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}\right)}}.

We consider two sub-cases according to the relative size of
(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}
:

Sub-case 1: When
(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}\leq\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}
:

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}.

Sub-case 2: When
(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}>\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}
:

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|}{n\sqrt{5D_{1}nd^{2}}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\left(\frac{1}{nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}-\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})-\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right).

Combining these sub-cases, when
\beta_{2}
is large enough such that
\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})\leq\frac{1}{4nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}
and
k
is large enough such that
\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq\frac{1}{4nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\left\{\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|}{2nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}-\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}-\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}.

Case (b): When
|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|<\frac{4\sqrt{2}\Delta_{k}}{(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{1}n}d}
:

In this case:

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|}{2nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}-\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{(1-\beta_{2})D_{1}n^{2}d^{2}\sqrt{5}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}

\displaystyle-\frac{8\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})}{(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{1}n}d}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}-\frac{8\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}C_{7}}{(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{1}n}d}\frac{1}{k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\left\{\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|}{2nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}-\frac{C_{10}}{\sqrt{k}},

where we define:

C_{10}=\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{(1-\beta_{2})D_{1}n^{2}d^{2}\sqrt{5}}+\frac{8\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+C_{7})}{(1-\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{1}n}d}.

(6.47)

Taking expectations and combining cases (a) and (b), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|}{2nd\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}-\frac{C_{11}}{\sqrt{k}},

where

C_{11}=C_{10}+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}},

(6.48)

and
C_{10}
,
C_{7}
are defined in (
6.47
), (
6.40
), respectively.

Using the relation
d(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}\geq\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|^{2}_{2}
and
d|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\geq\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{1}\geq\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{k}(v_{\alpha,k})}}-\left(\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})+\frac{C_{7}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k})|\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{nd\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2nd^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\delta_{12}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}-\frac{C_{11}}{\sqrt{k}}.

This completes the proof for (
6.44
). Finally, when
D_{1}=0
, i.e., when the bounded 2nd-order moment condition holds, we can arrive at a similar conclusion subject to some changes in the constant terms. The proof under
D_{1}=0
is strictly simpler than the current proof, as it reduces to the bounded gradient case. We complete the whole proof for Lemma
4.2
. ∎

7
Proof of the Convergence Results in Theorem
3.1

Now we present the proof for Theorem
3.1
. Based on the descent Lemma and the telescoping sum, by Lemma
4.2
we have the following relation. Let
T_{\min}
be sufficiently large and satisfy the conditions of Lemma
4.2
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\eta_{k}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{nd\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2nd^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{k}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle

(7.1)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f(z_{T_{\min}})-\mathbb{E}f(z_{T+1})+\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{L}{2}\mathbb{E}\|z_{k+1}-z_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f(z_{T_{\min}})-f^{*}+\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{L}{2}\mathbb{E}\|z_{k+1}-z_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

where
C
is defined in (
6.46
).

From (
B.5
) and the relation
z_{k+1}-z_{k}=\frac{x_{k+1}-x_{k}-\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k-n+1}-x_{k-n})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|z_{k+1}-z_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\left(\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}+\beta_{1}^{n}\|x_{k-n+1}-x_{k-n}\|_{2}\right)^{2}

(7.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{d(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}})}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\eta_{k}^{2}+\beta_{1}^{2n}\eta_{k-n}^{2}+2\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k}\eta_{k-n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k}^{2}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\cdot\frac{d^{2}(1-\beta_{1})^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})\left(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right)^{2}}\cdot\left(1+\beta_{1}^{2n}(n+1)+2\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k}\cdot\frac{d^{2}(1-\beta_{1})^{2}\eta_{0}^{2}\left(1+\beta_{1}^{2n}(n+1)+2\beta_{1}^{n}\sqrt{n+1}\right)}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}(1-\beta_{2})\left(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right)^{2}}:=\frac{C_{z}}{k},

where the second last inequality holds because for
k\geq n+1
,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{k-n}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{n+1}}{\sqrt{k}}
.

Plug (
7.2
) into (
7.1
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{nd\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2nd^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{k}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}f(z_{T_{\min}})-f^{*}+\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{L}{2}\cdot\frac{C_{z}}{k}.

(7.3)

Using
\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\geq 2(\sqrt{T}-\sqrt{T_{\min}})
and
\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{1}{k}\leq\log\frac{T+1}{T_{\min}}
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{k\in[T_{\min},T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{nd\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2nd^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\displaystyle\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}+\left(C\eta_{0}+\frac{LC_{z}}{2}\right)\log\frac{T+1}{T_{\min}}+\mathbb{E}f(z_{T_{\min}})-f^{*}}{\sum_{k=T_{\min}}^{T}\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(C\eta_{0}+\frac{LC_{z}}{2}\right)\frac{\log T}{\sqrt{T}}+\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}.

for sufficiently large
T
.

After appropriate scaling, we have the following relation.
