\displaystyle\min_{k\in[1,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{D_{0}}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}}{2\sqrt{dD_{1}}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n^{1.5}d^{1.5}\sqrt{5}\left(C\eta_{0}+\frac{LC_{z}}{2}\right)\frac{\log T}{\sqrt{T}}+n^{1.5}d^{1.5}\sqrt{5}\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}},

(7.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\delta(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}).

This concludes the proof for Theorem
3.1
. ∎

8
Proof of the Divergence Results in Theorem
3.5

We now present the proof for the divergence result. We will primarily consider
a=1
, which keeps the results clean. Nevertheless, the same proof procedure applies to any finite positive
a>0
. We denote
x_{k,i}
as the value of
x
at the
k
-th outer loop and
i
-th inner loop. We consider cyclic update ordering where
f_{i}(x)
are sampled in the order of
f_{0}(x),f_{1}(x),\cdots,f_{n-1}(x)
within the
k
-th outer loop. We only present the proof for Adam under cyclic update ordering, which helps reveal the key insights. The proof for random sampling follows the same procedure and gives the similar conclusion, which we omit for brevity. Firstly, we prove the following claim:

Claim:
for any
n\geq 3
, there exists an orange region shown in Figure
5
(a) s.t., Adam with any
(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
combination in the orange region gives
x_{k+1,0}>1
as long as
x_{k,0}=1
.

Now let us prove the claim. For function (
3.1
), the update rule of Adam is shown as follows.

\displaystyle x_{k,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(x_{k,0}+\delta_{k,0}\right),\quad\delta_{k,0}=-\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{n(1-\beta_{1})+\beta_{1}m_{k-1,n-1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})n^{2}+\beta_{2}v_{k-1,n-1}}}\right),

(8.1)

\displaystyle x_{k,i+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(x_{k,i}+\delta_{k,i}\right),\quad i=1,\cdots,n-1;

(8.2)

where
\delta_{k,i}=-\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}(-1)\beta_{1}^{j}+(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{i}n+\beta_{1}^{i+1}m_{k-1,n-1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,i-1}}}\right).

We decompose the total movement
\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\delta_{k,i}
into three terms as follows.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\delta_{k,i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\underbrace{\left(-\frac{\beta_{1}m_{k-1,n-1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})n^{2}+\beta_{2}v_{k-1,n-1}}}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}m_{k-1,n-1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,0}}}-\cdots-\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}m_{k-1,n-1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,n-2}}}\right)}_{\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\underbrace{\left(\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,0}}}+\frac{(1-\beta_{1})+\beta_{1}(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,1}}}+\cdots+\frac{(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{j}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,n-2}}}\right)}_{\text{(II)}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\underbrace{\left(-\frac{n(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})n^{2}+\beta_{2}v_{k-1,n-1}}}-\frac{n(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,0}}}-\cdots-\frac{n(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{n-1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,n-2}}}\right)}_{\text{(III)}}.

We will show that for some
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
:
(I)
,
\text{(II)}>0
and
\text{(III)}<0
. In addition,
(I)
and
(II)
outweigh
(III)
, causing the divergence. First, we show that
m_{k-1,n-1}<0
when
\beta_{1}
is small, which implies (I)
>0
.

\displaystyle-m_{k-1,n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{j}-(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{n-1}n-\beta_{1}^{n}m_{k-2,n-1}=\left[(1-\beta_{1}^{n-1})-(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{n-1}n\right]\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left(\beta_{1}^{n}\right)^{j}.

when
\beta_{1}
is small, we have
(1-\beta_{1}^{n-1})>(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{n-1}n
, which implies
-m_{k-1,n-1}>0
. For these choices of
\beta_{1}
, we have
\text{(I)}>0
. Now we derive a lower bound for
(II)
.

(II)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1+\beta_{2}n^{2}}}+\frac{(1-\beta_{1})+\beta_{1}(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1+\beta_{2}^{2}n^{2}}}+\cdots+\frac{(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{j}}{\sqrt{1+\beta_{2}^{n-1}n^{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1+\beta_{2}n^{2}}}+\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\beta_{2}^{2}n^{2}}}+\cdots+\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{n-1}}{\sqrt{1+\beta_{2}^{n-1}n^{2}}}.

The inequality is due to the fact that
v_{k,0}\leq n^{2}
. Since
\beta_{2}^{j}n^{2}
is small when
\beta_{2}
is small and
j
is close to
n
, there exists some small
\beta_{2}
such that
\beta_{2}^{j}n^{2}\leq 0.1
for at least one
j<n
. For these small enough
\beta_{2}
, we keep the summand with
j\geq\log_{\beta_{2}}(0.1/n^{2})
and drop the rest. With basic calculus, we have

(II)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(n-1-\min\left\{n-1,\log_{\beta_{2}}(\frac{1}{10n^{2}})\right\}\right)\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{\min\left\{n-1,\log_{\beta_{2}}(\frac{1}{10n^{2}})\right\}}}{\sqrt{1+\max\left\{0.1,\beta_{2}^{n-1}n^{2}\right\}}}

Now we derive an upper bound for
|\text{(III)}|
.

\displaystyle|\text{(III)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{n(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})n^{2}+\beta_{2}v_{k-1,n-1}}}\right|+\left|\frac{n(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,0}}}\right|+\cdots+\left|\frac{n(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{n-1}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})+\beta_{2}v_{k,n-2}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\left(1+n(\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\beta_{1}^{j})\right)=\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}+\frac{\beta_{1}(1-\beta_{1}^{n-1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}n

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}+\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}n.

Further, we will use a small enough stepsize
\eta_{0}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{n}}
to ensure the iterates will stay in the linear region, thus the above relations hold for all iterates in the trajectory. In summary, the divergence happens if the following conditions hold:

{\bf(C1):}\left(n-1-\min\left\{n-1,\log_{\beta_{2}}(\frac{1}{10n^{2}})\right\}\right)\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{\min\left\{n-1,\log_{\beta_{2}}(\frac{1}{10n^{2}})\right\}}}{\sqrt{1+\max\left\{0.1,\beta_{2}^{n-1}n^{2}\right\}}}\geq\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}+\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}n;

(8.3)

{\bf(C2):}(1-\beta_{1}^{n-1})>(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{n-1}n;

(8.4)

{\bf(C3):}\eta_{0}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{n}}.

(8.5)

This concludes the proof on the divergence of Adam’s iterates and function values. The divergence of gradients can also be proved following a similar procedure by changing the initialization to
x<0
.
Finally, the above proof procedure applies to any finite positive
a>0
, not merely for
a=1
. When
a=1/(n-1)^{2}
, our counter-example (
3.1
) satisfies
D_{1}=2n^{2}
,
D_{0}=0
, so the divergence happens for arbitrary function class
\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
D_{1}\geq 2n^{2}
and
D_{0}\geq 0
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
3.5
. ∎

With the help of
NumPy
, we visualize the region where
{\bf(C1)}
and
{\bf(C2)}
hold. The results are shown in Figure
8
. We use orange color to indicate the region where
{\bf(C1)}
holds. White color is used for the counterpart. As for
{\bf(C2)}
, we use the gray vertical line to indicate
the line where
(1-\beta_{1}^{n-1})=(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{n-1}n
. Note that there are two solutions to this equation: one solution is
\beta_{1}=1
and the other solution lies in
0<\beta_{1}<1
, this is why there are two vertical lines in the figure.
{\bf(C2)}
holds on the left-hand side of the left gray vertical line.

(a)

n=5

(b)

n=10

(c)

n=50

(d)

n=100

Figure 8:

This figure illustrates the region where both
{\bf(C1)}
and
{\bf(C2)}
in (
8.3
) and (
8.4
) hold. The orange color indicates the region where
{\bf(C1)}
holds. White color is used for the counterpart. The gray vertical lines are used to indicate
the boundary of
{\bf(C2)}
. Note that there are two solutions to the equation in
{\bf(C2)}
: one solution is
\beta_{1}=1
and the other solution lies in
0<\beta_{1}<1
, this is why there are two vertical lines in the figure.
{\bf(C2)}
holds on the left hand side of the left gray vertical line.
The region is plotted by solving condition (
8.3
), (
8.4
), (
8.5
) in
NumPy
.

The intersection of the two regions will be the region where Adam diverges, which is actually the orange region in Figure
8
. The size of the divergence region increases with
n
.

Relation with
\gamma_{1}(n)
in Theorem
3.1
.

According to Theorem
3.1
,
\gamma_{1}(n)
is at least in the order of
1-\mathcal{O}(n^{-5})
. Combining with Figure
8
. It is not hard to see that
\gamma_{1}(n)
is always larger than the upper boundary of the orange region, so there is no contradiction.

9
Limitations and Future Directions

•

More fine-grained characterization on the critical boundary.
Integrating our divergence and convergence theories, one can conclude that there exists (at least) one critical boundary
(\beta_{1}^{*},\beta_{2}^{*})
that demarcates the di-convergence phase transition. However, we have not fully determined the precise number and the shape of the boundar(-ies). Our experimental results in Fig.
1
(c, d) suggest that there exists only one boundary, which likely resembles the shape of the blue region in Fig.
1
(b). We only point out its existence here and leave a more precise characterization as a future direction.

•

Adam v.s. SGD.
In this work, we focus on the fundamental issue of convergence. One intriguing question is to
verify the advantage of Adam over SGD. Identifying when and why Adam converges faster serves as an independent research topic, and we leave as future investigation.

•

Generalized Lipschitz conditions.
A recent line of theoretical work relaxes the standard Lipschitz condition in Assumption
2.1
to some generalized Lipschitz conditions (e.g.,
(
91
;
51
;
83
)
). As mentioned earlier in Section
2.1
, the generalized Lipschitz conditions primarily help refine the
quantitative
analysis of Adam, e.g., yielding a sharper convergence rate. Meanwhile, the core focus of this work is a more basic topic: characterizing the
qualitative
behavior of Adam—such as phase transition from divergence to convergence. For this purpose, the standard Lipschitz condition is already adequate. Nevertheless, generalizing the Lipschitz condition is a theoretically intriguing direction for future research.

•

Tighter bounds.
Finally, we note that in our convergence upper bounds, the
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
term has multiplicative constants of order
\mathcal{O}(d^{4}n^{4.5})
, where
d
is the problem dimension
d
and
n
is the number of mini-batches. Similarly, the threshold of
\beta_{2}
is in the order of
n^{5}
and
n^{5.5}
. These powers are not claimed tight, and it is possible to reduce these dependencies via a more fine-grained analysis. We leave it as an interesting future direction.

•

Optimal combination of
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
.
Our results point out the existence of a safe region of
(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
, but we have not yet identified which combination of
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
yields the optimal performance within the safe region. Recent works
(
96
;
66
)
report that
\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}
(when both are large enough) usually brings optimal performance, and they provide an initial explanation. This finding is further investigated in recent works
(
31
;
13
)
. Identifying the optimal hyperparameter is an independent topic that requires substantially more effort. We leave it as an important future direction.

10
Conclusions

In this work, we explore the convergence of Adam. When
\beta_{2}
is large, we
prove that Adam converges
with any
\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}
.
When
\beta_{2}
is small, we further show that Adam can diverge to infinity for a wide range of
\beta_{1}
. The critical boundary of the phase transition
(\beta_{1}^{*},\beta_{2}^{*})
is problem-dependent, and in particular, depends on batch size. Our results provide rigorous theoretical groundings for Adam optimizer. These results also provide
practical suggestions on how to tune
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
.

Appendix

Table of Contents for the Appendix

Appendix A
More Discussions

A.1
More Related Works

On the Variants of Adam.

Ever since
71
pointed out the divergence issue of Adam, one active line of work has tried to design new variants of Adam that can be proved to converge. For instance,
99
;
32
;
16
;
14

replace the constant hyperparameters by iterate-dependent ones e.g.
\beta_{1k}
or
\beta_{2k}
. AMSGrad
(
71
)
and AdaFom
(
16
)
modify
\{v_{k}\}
to be a non-decreasing sequence.
43
further analyze the convergence of AMSGrad by relaxing the Lipschitz-gradient condition. However, their analysis requires extra conditions on both bounded gradient and bounded domain.
AdaBound
(
60
)
imposes lower and upper bounds on
\{v_{k}\}
to prevent the effective stepsize from vanishing or exploding.
98
also adopt a new estimate of
v_{k}
to correct the bias.
There are also attempts to combine Adam with Nesterov momentum
(
29
)
as well as warm-up techniques
(
57
)
. Padam
(
15
)
also introduce a partial adaptive parameter to improve the generalization performance.
There are also some works providing theoretical analysis on the variants of Adam. For instance,
97
study the convergence of AdaGrad and AMSGrad under bounded gradient condition.
32
study the asymptotic behavior of a subclass of adaptive gradient methods from landscape point of view. Their analysis applies to a sub-class of Adam variants with
\beta_{1}=0
and
\beta_{2}
increasing along the iterates (it could also be understood as RMSProp with increasing
\beta_{2}
).
2
study AMSGrad and two other variants of Adam. Their analysis requires both bounded gradient and bounded domain assumptions, and does not cover the original Adam.
43
analyze the convergence of AMSGrad by relaxing the Lipschitz-gradient condition. However, their analysis requires extra conditions on both bounded gradient and bounded domain.

Another line of theoretical work imposes extra conditions on Adam’s trajectory, which implicitly modifies the update rules of Adam.

20
analyze RMSProp and Adam, but they assume the sign of all stochastic gradients to keep the same. This requires additional projection steps in every iteration, and the resulting algorithm is no longer vanilla Adam.
5
provide two proofs for Adam. The first proof considers
(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
close to
(1,1)
and it requires bounded gradient and bounded iterates assumptions. As discussed in the paper, we need
“a projection step on a compact set … to ensure the boundedness of the estimates”
. This projection step changes the update rules of Adam, and more importantly, it eliminates the possibility of divergence a priori, and thus did not fully capture the behaviors of vanilla Adam. The second proof also requires bounded gradient assumption and it replaces the constant hyperparameters by iterate-dependent ones
\beta_{1k}
or
\beta_{2k}
under certain rules. This also changes the update rules of Adam.

40
and
35
propose novel and simple frameworks to analyze Adam-family with large
\beta_{1}
.
Yet, they require the effective stepsize of Adam to be bounded in certain interval, i.e.,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{k}}+\epsilon}\in[C_{l},C_{u}]
. This boundedness condition is also imposed for RMSProp in
(
89
)
. This boundedness condition changes Adam into AdaBound
(
60
)
, and thus they cannot explain the divergence-convergence phase transition on the original Adam.

To summarize, these works study the modified versions of Adam or implicitly impose extra operations on Adam (such as projection), which changes the update rules of Adam.
Additionally, they all (including those for new variants) require bounded gradient assumptions, which prevents the potential divergence a priori. In contrast, we do not require such assumptions and prove the existence of divergence-convergence phase transition when changing
(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
.

Understanding Adam under specialized settings.

There is a line of work on understanding Adam in specialized settings such as quadratic functions (e.g.,
(
18
;
19
;
93
)
), strongly convex functions
(
23
)
, and deterministic and locally strongly convex functions
(
25
)
.
45
study the evolution of a newly defined condition number along Adam’s trajectory on large-batch two-layer linear network. There are also theoretical works analyzing the dynamics of Adam under continuous-time approximation via ODE or SDE methods (e.g.,
(
18
;
62
;
26
;
50
;
17
;
24
;
54
)
).
Different from these works, we do not adopt the continuous approximation and analyze the original (and thus discrete) Adam on generic non-convex functions.
There is also excellent work on understanding the benefit of AdamW over Adam under deterministic (full-batch) settings
(
85
)
, which serves as an orthogonal research topic to this work.

A.2
More Discussions on the Bounded Variance Condition (
2.3
)

Here, we provide more discussion on why the classical “bounded variance” condition
(
2.3
) is too restricted, and why it is meaningful to relax it to a more general form, such as Assumption
2.2
. Consider a convex quadratic minimization problem
(
10
)
:

\operatorname*{minimize}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\quad f(x)=\frac{1}{2n}\|Ax-b\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(a_{i}^{\top}x-b_{i})^{2}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}f_{i}(x),

(A.1)

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
a_{i}^{\top}
denotes the
i
-th row of
A
and we assume
A
has rank at least
2
. For (
A.1
), we will show that bounded variance condition (
2.3
) fails, but Assumption
2.2
holds.

Bounded variance condition (
2.3
) fails on (
A.1
).

Let
r(x):=Ax-b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with entries
r_{i}(x)=a_{i}^{\top}x-b_{i}
. For the decomposition in
(
2.4
), we have

\nabla f_{i}(x)=(a_{i}^{\top}x-b_{i})a_{i}=r_{i}(x)a_{i},\qquad\nabla f(x)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\nabla f_{i}(x)=\frac{1}{n}A^{\top}r(x).

Plugging into (
2.3
) gives

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x)-\nabla f(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|r_{i}(x)a_{i}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\|A^{\top}r(x)\|^{2}.

(A.2)

Assume
\mathrm{rank}(A)\geq 2
, so there exist two indices
p\neq q
such that
a_{p}
and
a_{q}
are not collinear.
Pick
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

a_{p}^{\top}u=1,\qquad a_{q}^{\top}u=-1,

(A.3)

which is feasible since
a_{p},a_{q}
are linearly independent. Consider the ray
x(t)=x_{0}+tu
for any fixed
x_{0}
.
Then

r_{p}(x(t))=r_{p}(x_{0})+t,\qquad r_{q}(x(t))=r_{q}(x_{0})-t,

and for every
i\notin\{p,q\}
,
r_{i}(x(t))=r_{i}(x_{0})+t\,a_{i}^{\top}u
grows at most linearly in
t
.

Using (
A.2
) and keeping only the two terms
p,q
yields the lower bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x(t))-\nabla f(x(t))\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\left(\|r_{p}(x(t))a_{p}\|^{2}+\|r_{q}(x(t))a_{q}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\|A^{\top}r(x(t))\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left((r_{p}(x_{0})+t)^{2}\|a_{p}\|^{2}+(r_{q}(x_{0})-t)^{2}\|a_{q}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\|A^{\top}r(x(t))\|^{2}\right).

(A.4)

The first two terms in (
A.4
) grow like
(\|a_{p}\|^{2}+\|a_{q}\|^{2})t^{2}
as
|t|\to\infty
.
Meanwhile,
\|A^{\top}r(x(t))\|^{2}=\|A^{\top}(Ax(t)-b)\|^{2}
is a quadratic function of
t
as well, but the
coefficient in front of
t^{2}
is
\|A^{\top}Au\|^{2}
.
Because
a_{p}^{\top}u=1
and
a_{q}^{\top}u=-1
, we have
Au
has at least two nonzero coordinates of opposite sign,
and in particular
\|Au\|^{2}\geq 2
.

Therefore, along
x(t)
the variance expression in (
A.2
) is a
nonconstant quadratic polynomial in
t
with a strictly positive leading coefficient, and hence is unbounded above as
|t|\to\infty
. Consequently, there is no
finite constant
D_{0}
such that (
2.3
) holds for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

To sum up, for the quadratic example (
2.4
) with
\mathrm{rank}(A)\geq 2
, the left-hand side of
(
2.3
) can grow on the order of
\|x\|^{2}
, so the bounded variance condition fails in general.

Assumption
2.2
holds with finite constants.

We next show that Assumption
2.2
can still hold for (
2.4
). Let

W:=\mathrm{diag}(\|a_{0}\|^{2},\dots,\|a_{n-1}\|^{2})
. Since
\nabla f_{i}(x)=r_{i}(x)a_{i}
,

\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|\nabla f_{i}(x)\|^{2}=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}r_{i}(x)^{2}\|a_{i}\|^{2}=r(x)^{\top}Wr(x).

(A.5)

Moreover,

\|\nabla f(x)\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\|A^{\top}r(x)\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}r(x)^{\top}(AA^{\top})\,r(x).

(A.6)

Decompose
r(x)=r_{\parallel}(x)+r_{\perp}
with

r_{\parallel}(x)\in\mathrm{range}(A)
and
r_{\perp}\in\mathrm{null}(A^{\top})
. Since
r(x)=Ax-b
and
Ax\in\mathrm{range}(A)
,
the orthogonal component is constant in
x
:

r_{\perp}=-\Pi_{\mathrm{null}(A^{\top})}b,\qquad A^{\top}r_{\perp}=0.

Using
(u+v)^{\top}W(u+v)\leq 2u^{\top}Wu+2v^{\top}Wv
and (
A.5
) and (
A.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|\nabla f_{i}(x)\|^{2}=r^{\top}Wr

\displaystyle\leq 2r_{\parallel}^{\top}Wr_{\parallel}+2r_{\perp}^{\top}Wr_{\perp}

(A.7)

\displaystyle\leq 2c\,r_{\parallel}^{\top}(AA^{\top})r_{\parallel}+2r_{\perp}^{\top}Wr_{\perp}=2cn^{2}\|\nabla f(x)\|^{2}+2r_{\perp}^{\top}Wr_{\perp},

where

c:=\sup_{u\in\mathrm{range}(A),\,u\neq 0}\frac{u^{\top}Wu}{u^{\top}(AA^{\top})u}\;<\;\infty.

(A.8)

Finiteness of
c
follows because
AA^{\top}
is positive definite on
\mathrm{range}(A)
, hence the generalized
Rayleigh quotient in (
A.8
) is bounded.

Thus Assumption
2.2
holds with

D_{1}=2cn^{2},\qquad D_{0}=2\,r_{\perp}^{\top}Wr_{\perp}=2\,\big(\Pi_{\mathrm{null}(A^{\top})}b\big)^{\top}W\,\big(\Pi_{\mathrm{null}(A^{\top})}b\big),

(A.9)

both finite constants depending only on
(A,b)
. We also mention a special case here: if
b\in\mathrm{range}(A)
, then
r_{\perp}=0
and (
A.9
) gives
D_{0}=0
.

In conclusion, for the quadratic problem (
2.4
), Assumption
2.2
can hold with finite
(D_{1},D_{0})
, while the bounded variance condition (
2.3
) fails.

Appendix B
Some More Notations and Useful Lemmas

Some More Notations.

For Adam under random shuffling (Algorithm
2
), we denote
x_{k,i},m_{k,i},v_{k,i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
as the value of
x,m,v
in the
k
-th outer loop and
i
-th inner loop. Further, we denote
x_{l,k,i},m_{l,k,i},v_{l,k,i}\in\mathbb{R}
as the
l
-th component of
x_{k,i},m_{k,i},v_{k,i}
. Further, we will use
\tau_{k,i}
to index the
i
-th randomly chosen batch in the
k
-th epoch. In this sense, we denote
\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x)
as
\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{l}}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x)
.

For each epoch
k\geq 1
, we use
\mathbb{E}_{k}[\cdot]
as a shorthand for the conditional expectation given the history up to (and including) the epoch-start iterate
x_{k,0}
, but excluding the permutation
\{\tau_{k,0},\ldots,\tau_{k,n-1}\}
sampled in epoch
k
. In other words, the expectation is conditioned on the initial states (
m_{1,-1}
,
v_{1,-1}
, and
x_{1,0}
), and all past permutations
\{\tau_{s,0},\ldots,\tau_{s,n-1}\}
for
s=1,\ldots,k-1
.
Similarly,
\mathbb{E}_{k-1}[\cdot]
denotes the conditional expectation given the entire history up to (and including)
x_{k-1,0}
, but excluding the permutation in epoch
k-1
.

We abuse the notation of
\alpha
as follows: for
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x)
, we define
\alpha:=\arg\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x)|
; for
\partial_{\alpha}f(x)
, we define
\alpha:=\arg\max_{l}|\partial_{l}f(x)|
.
Similarly for
m_{\alpha,k,i}
and
v_{\alpha,k,i}
.

We further define the following constants, which will be repeatedly used in the analysis of Adam under both with-replacement sampling (Algorithm
1
) and random shuffling (Algorithm
2
).

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}:=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{L\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}},

\displaystyle\quad Q_{k}:=\frac{32(n+1)\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n\right).

(B.1)

Some Useful Lemmas for Theorem
3.1
and
3.3
.

We now prove the following Lemma
B.2
, Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
B.4
, which will be repeatedly used later.

Lemma B.1
.

For any
\beta\in(0,1)
, we have

\left(1-\beta\right)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\beta^{j-1}=1,\quad\left(1-\beta\right)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}j\beta^{j-1}=\frac{1}{1-\beta},\quad\left(1-\beta\right)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}j^{2}\beta^{j-1}=\frac{1+\beta}{\left(1-\beta\right)^{2}}.

Proof.

The proof only involves basic calculation, we omit the proof here.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Consider Algorithm
1
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
l\in[d]
,
i\in[n]
, if
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
, then we have

\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k+1})-\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})\right|\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{L\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}:=\Delta_{k}.

(B.2)

Similarly, for Algorithm
2
, we have the following results under the same condition:

\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,i+1})-\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,i})\right|\leq\Delta_{k}.

(B.3)

Proof.

Here, we present the proof of (
B.2
). The proof of (
B.3
) is done following the same procedure.

\displaystyle\left|x_{l,k+1}-x_{l,k}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\frac{|m_{l,k}|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}

(B.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{k}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{j=0}^{k}\beta_{1}^{j}\frac{|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{j=0}^{k}\beta_{1}^{j}\frac{|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|}{|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{j}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{=}

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right)^{j}\leq\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}.

(B.5)

Note that (i) holds due to
v_{l,k}\geq(1-\beta_{2})\beta_{2}^{j}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})^{2}
. (ii) clearly holds when
|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|>0
.
When
|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|=0
, the summand in (
B.4
) equals 0 under non-zero initialization
v\succ 0
or
\epsilon>0
, which keeps Adam well-defined. Hence, the inequality in (ii) holds trivially.
In either case, we arrive at the final result (
B.5
), and the final result is independent of initialization
v
or
\epsilon
. We further remark that this is the only place in the analysis where we use the condition
v\succ 0
.

We conclude the proof of Lemma
B.2
by applying the Lipschitz condition in Assumption
2.1
.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Consider Algorithm
1
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
,
l\in[d]
,
i\in[n]
and
j\in[k-2]
, when

|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}j\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}
, we have

(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j}))^{2}\geq\frac{(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}}{2}.

(B.6)

Similarly, for Algorithm
2
, when

|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}jn\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}
, for any
i^{\prime}\in[n]
, we have

(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j,i^{\prime}}))^{2}\geq\frac{(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{2}.

(B.7)

Proof.

Here, we present the proof of (
B.6
). The proof of (
B.7
) is done following the same procedure.

\displaystyle(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j}))^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j})-\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})+\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}-2|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j})-\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})||\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}\left(1-\frac{2|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j})-\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|}{|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|}\right).

By Lemma
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j})-\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-i}.

(B.8)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{2j\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k-1}}\overset{}{\leq}\frac{2j\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}},

where (i) is due to

\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k-i}}\leq\int_{k-j-1}^{k-1}\frac{dt}{\sqrt{t}}=\frac{2j}{\sqrt{k-j-1}+\sqrt{k-1}}\leq\frac{2j}{\sqrt{k-1}}.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j}))^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}\left(1-\frac{4j\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\sqrt{k}}\right).

So when
|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}j\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}
, we have
(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j}))^{2}\geq\frac{(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k}))^{2}}{2}
, which concludes the proof.∎

Lemma B.4
.

Consider Algorithm
1
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
,
l\in[d]
,
i\in[n]
and
j\in[k-1]
, we have the following two results:

|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right),

(B.9)

where
\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|:=\max_{l\in[d]}\left|\partial_{l}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|
.

Similarly, for Algorithm
2
, we have the following results under the same condition.

|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})|\leq i\Delta_{k}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right),

(B.10)

where
\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|:=\max_{l\in[d]}\left|\partial_{l}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|
.

Proof.

Here, we present the proof of (
B.9
). The proof of (
B.10
) is done via the same procedure.

\displaystyle|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k})|

(B.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}|\partial_{l^{\prime}}f_{i}(x_{k})|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Cauchy\textendash Schwarz inequality}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sqrt{n}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{d}\sqrt{\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left|\partial_{l^{\prime}}f_{i}(x_{k})\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Cauchy\textendash Schwarz inequality}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sqrt{n}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left|\partial_{l^{\prime}}f_{i}(x_{k})\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Assumption \ref{assum2}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sqrt{n}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{D_{1}\|\nabla f(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}+D_{0}}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\sqrt{\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right).

(B.12)

The proof is complete. As a side remark, we further note that if
D_{1}=0
(i.e., assume bounded 2nd-order moment), (
B.11
) can be further simplified using
\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left|\partial_{l^{\prime}}f_{i}(x_{k})\right|^{2}\leq D_{0}
, and the final result would be
|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j}}(x_{k-j})|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{j}\Delta_{k-t}+\sqrt{ndD_{0}}
. This is a special case of (
B.12
) and it will make the subsequent analysis strictly simpler since we get rid of the dependency on
\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k}\right)\right|
. The simplified analysis will have strictly fewer error terms and can be done by following the same procedure as the current proof. In our analysis, we do not restrict
D_{1}
to be
0
.

∎

Lemma B.5
.

Consider Algorithm
2
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
,
l\in[d]
,
i\in[n]
and
k\geq 4
large enough such that
\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}}\beta_{1}^{(k-1)n+1}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
, we have

\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}|m_{l,k,i}|\leq C_{m,1}\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{C_{m,2}}{\sqrt{k}},

(B.13)

\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}|m_{l,k-1,i}|\leq\tilde{C}_{m,1}\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{\tilde{C}_{m,2}}{\sqrt{k}},

(B.14)

where

C_{m,1}=2n,\quad C_{m,2}=\frac{n(n+1)\Delta_{1}}{2(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{n}}+\beta_{1}\frac{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}{(1-\beta_{1})^{2}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\|\nabla f_{i}(x_{1,0})\|_{1},

\tilde{C}_{m,1}=n,\hskip 9.24994pt\tilde{C}_{m,2}=\frac{n}{1-\beta_{1}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}n(n-1)\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\|\nabla f_{i}(x_{1,0})\|_{1}.

Proof.

Lemma
B.5
can be proved by repeatedly applying Lemma
B.4
. The proof is straightforward and we omit the proof for brevity.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Lemma
6.1

Proof.

We now prove Lemma
6.1
. We discuss the following two cases.

Case 1: when
\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\geq\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}
:

when
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\leq 0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\overset{(\ref{eq:v_k-1_geq_v_k})}{\leq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

When
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})>0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\overset{}{\leq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}.

In conclusion, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\max\left\{\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\left[\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-1\right],\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}-1\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\frac{\left|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\right|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\underbrace{\left(\left[1-\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}-1\right)}_{\delta_{5,1}(\beta_{2})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{eq:v_k-1_geq_v_k})}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\frac{\left|\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\right|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\underbrace{\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}_{\delta_{5,2}(\beta_{2})}\delta_{5,1}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{eq_concentrate_v_epsilon_given}) (\ref{upperbound_of_f_over_v_epsilon})}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\underbrace{\frac{2\sqrt{2n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}}}_{\delta_{5,3}(\beta_{2})}\delta_{5,1}(\beta_{2})\delta_{5,2}(\beta_{2}).

Denote
\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})=\delta_{5,1}(\beta_{2})\delta_{5,2}(\beta_{2})\delta_{5,3}(\beta_{2})
.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})-\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{k-1}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\delta_{5}(\beta_{2}).

Case 2: when
\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\leq\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}
:

when
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})\geq 0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\overset{\text{\eqref{eq:v_k-1_geq_v_k}}}{\geq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})\left(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}-(1-\beta_{2})16n}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

When
\partial_{l}f(x_{k})<0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}\overset{}{\geq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}.

Following the same strategy as in Case 1, we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}-\delta_{5}(\beta_{2}),

which further implies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})-\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}-\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{\Delta_{k-1}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}-\delta_{5}(\beta_{2}).

Combining
Case 1
and
Case 2
together, we have

\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}\right|\leq\frac{\Delta_{k-1}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\delta_{5}(\beta_{2}).

Based on the above inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-n})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\Delta_{k-1}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1}}}+\frac{\Delta_{k-2}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-2}}}+\cdots+\frac{\Delta_{k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}\right)+n\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\Delta_{k-1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}v_{l,k-n}}}+\frac{\Delta_{k-2}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}v_{l,k-n}}}+\cdots+\frac{\Delta_{k-n}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}v_{l,k-n}}}\right)+n\delta_{5}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\frac{\Delta_{k-n}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-n}}}+n\delta_{5}(\beta_{2}).

The proof of Lemma
6.1
is now complete.

∎

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
3.3

Before delving into the proof of Theorem
3.3
, we first present some additional technical lemmas that will be used in the proof.
We recall that
v_{k,-1}=v_{k-1,n-1}
, as defined in Algorithm
2
.

Lemma D.1
.

Consider Algorithm
2
. Assume
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+1
and
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k,0}):=\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
, we have

\frac{\left(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right)^{2}}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}\leq\frac{4n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}.

(D.1)

The proof of Lemma
D.1
is in Appendix
D.1
. Now we present the concentration inequalities of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}
.

Lemma D.2
.

Consider Algorithm
2
. Assume
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+1
and
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k,0}):=\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
, and for any
i\in[n]
, we have

\left(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-\beta_{2})n}(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{2})\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}},

(D.2)

The proof of Lemma
D.2
can be seen in Appendix
D.2
.

Lemma D.3
.

Consider Algorithm
2
. Assume
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+1
and
\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|\geq\frac{16n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
, and for any

i=-1,0,\cdots,n-2
, we have

\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,i}}}\leq\left(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-\beta_{2})n}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}

(D.3)

The proof of Lemma
D.3
can be seen in Appendix
D.3
.

Lemma D.4
.

Consider Algorithm
2
. Assume
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+1
. Assume
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k,0}):=\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq\frac{16n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
,
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k-1,0}):=\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-1,0})|\geq\frac{16n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k-1}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
, …,
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k-j,0}):=\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-j,0})|\geq\frac{16n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k-j}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
, then for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
, we have

\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-j,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\leq\frac{j}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{nj}}}\frac{n\Delta_{(k-j)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+j\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2}),

(D.4)

where
\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})=4n\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1+\left[1-\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is a constant that goes to 0 when
\beta_{2}
goes to 1.

The proof of Lemma
D.4
can be seen in Appendix
D.4
. The main body of the proof of Theorem
3.3
is then presented in Appendix
D.7
.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
D.1

We start with a lower bound for
v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}
.

\displaystyle v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,n-1})\right)^{2}+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{n{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(k-1)}}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{1,0}}(x_{1,0})\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\beta_{2}^{nj}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j,i}}(x_{k-j,i})\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\beta_{2}^{nj}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j,i}}(x_{k-j,i})\right)^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}(\tau_{k-j,i}=\alpha)\right)

where
\alpha=\arg\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|
. Since
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+1
, we further have:

\displaystyle v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil}\beta_{2}^{nj}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j,i}}(x_{k-j,i})\right)^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}(\tau_{k-j,i}=\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\beta_{2})}{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil}\beta_{2}^{nj}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-j,i}}(x_{k,0})\right)^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}(\tau_{k-j,i}=\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{2}\cdot\beta_{2}^{n}\cdot\frac{1-\beta_{2}^{n\left\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\right\rceil}}{1-\beta_{2}^{n}}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k,0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{2}^{n}}{4}\cdot\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{1-\beta_{2}^{n}}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k,0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(iii)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{2}^{n}}{4n}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k,0})\right)^{2}

where (i) is obtained by applying Lemma
B.3
, whose condition
\partial_{l}f_{\alpha}(x_{k,0}):=\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\right)
is satisfied.
(ii) is due to
1-\beta_{2}^{n\left\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\right\rceil}\geq\frac{1}{2}
; (iii) is due to
\frac{1-\beta_{2}}{1-\beta_{2}^{n}}\geq\frac{1}{n}
. Rearrange the inequality and we conclude the proof for Lemma
D.1
.

We further comment on one difference between Lemma
D.1
and the concentration results for the with-replacement sampling version, i.e., Lemma
4.1
. Here, under random shuffling, index
\alpha
will occur once and only once within one epoch, while this is not guaranteed under with-replacement sampling. As a result, the conclusion of Lemma
D.1
is deterministic, while the conclusion in Lemma
4.1
is not.

∎

D.2
Proof of Lemma
D.2

The upper bound in (
D.2
) is straightforward due to
v_{l,k,i}\geq\beta_{2}^{n}v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}
. We now prove the lower bound in (
D.2
).

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{|v_{l,k,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|}{{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-\frac{|v_{l,k,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|}{2v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}\right),

where
(i):\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+x}}\geq 1-\frac{x}{2}
. Now we provide an upper bound of
|v_{l,k,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|
.

\displaystyle|v_{l,k,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|(1-\beta_{2})\left(\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})\right)^{2}+\beta_{2}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,i-1})\right)^{2}+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}}(x_{k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})\right)^{2}\right)-(1-\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i+1}})v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|(1-\beta_{2})\left((\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}})+\beta_{2}(\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,i-1})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}})+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}(\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}}(x_{k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|+\beta_{2}\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,i-1})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}}(x_{k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|\right)

Recall

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})-\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})\right|+\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle n\Delta_{k}+\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})\right|,

so we have

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(n\Delta_{k})^{2}+2n\Delta_{k}\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})\right|+\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 4\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|^{2},

where (i) is due to
\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}>n\Delta_{k}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle|v_{l,k,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|+\beta_{2}\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,i-1})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,1}}(x_{k,1})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})\left(\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})\right)^{2}+v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|+\beta_{2}\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,i-1})\right)^{2}+v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}\left|\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,1}}(x_{k,1})\right)^{2}+v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{2})n\left(4\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|^{2}+v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right).

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-\frac{|v_{l,k,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|}{2v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-\frac{(1-\beta_{2})n\left(4\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|^{2}+v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\right)}{2v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_f_over_v}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{2})\right).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
D.2
. ∎

D.3
Proof of Lemma
D.3

The lower bound in (
D.3
) is straightforward due to
v_{l,k-1,i}\leq\frac{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}{\beta_{2}^{n}}
. Regarding the upper bound in (
D.2
), we consider

\displaystyle v_{l,k-1,i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\cdot\frac{v_{l,k-1,i}}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\cdot\frac{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}+v_{l,k-1,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\cdot\left(1-\frac{|v_{l,k-1,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}\right).

The rest of the proof follows the same procedure as the proof of Lemma
D.2
. Firstly, one can show that: when
\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|\geq\frac{16n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
, we have

\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,j}}(x_{k-1,j})\right)^{2}\leq 4\max_{i}\left(\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right)^{2}.

(D.5)

Therefore, we have
the following results for
i=-1,0,\cdots,n-2

\displaystyle\frac{\left|v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}-v_{l,k-1,i}\right|}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{(1-\beta_{2})}{{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left[\left(\left(\partial_{l}f_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{k-1,n-1}}}(x_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k-1,n-1}})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k-1,i}\right)+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{n-i{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2}}\left(\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,i+1})\right)^{2}-v_{l,k-1,i}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\beta_{2})}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}\left[\left(\left(\partial_{l}f_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{k-1,n-1}}}(x_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k-1,n-1}})\right)^{2}+v_{l,k-1,i}\right)+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{n-i{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2}}\left(\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,i+1})\right)^{2}+v_{l,k-1,i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq_k-1_to_k}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\beta_{2})}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}(1+\cdots+\beta_{2}^{n-i{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2}})\left(4\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|^{2}+v_{l,k-1,i}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-\beta_{2})n}\left(\frac{16n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}\right),

where the last inequality is due to
v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\geq\beta_{2}^{n{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}-i}v_{l,k-1,i}

for
i=-1,0,\cdots,n-2
,
and Lemma
D.1
(note that the condition of Lemma
D.1
is satisfied when
\max_{i}\left|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})\right|\geq\frac{16n\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}
). In summary, we have

\displaystyle v_{l,k-1,i}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\left(1-\frac{|v_{l,k-1,i}-v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|}{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left(\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}\right)\right).

Rearrange the inequality and we conclude the proof for Lemma
D.3
. ∎

D.4
Proof of Lemma
D.4

We first prove the result for
j=1
. We discuss the following two cases.

Case 1: when
\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\geq\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}
:

when
\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\leq 0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_concentrate_v_k-1}}}{\leq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

When
\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})>0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_concentrate_v_k-1}}}{\leq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}.

In conclusion, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\max\left\{\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left[\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-1\right],\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\frac{\left|\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\right|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1+\left[1-\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_concentrate_v_k-1}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\frac{\left|\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\right|}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1+\left[1-\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_f_over_v}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\sqrt{\frac{4n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1+\left[1-\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2}),

where
\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2}):=\sqrt{\frac{4n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1+\left[1-\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is a constant that goes to 0 when
\beta_{2}
goes to 1. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})-\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\tilde{\delta_{1}}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{n\Delta_{(k-1)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\tilde{\delta_{1}}(\beta_{2}).

Case 2: when
\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\leq\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}
:

when
\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\geq 0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\overset{\text{\eqref{lemma_concentrate_v_k-1}}}{\geq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16n+1}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

When
\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})<0
, we have

\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\overset{}{\geq}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}.

Following the same strategy as in Case 1, we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2}),

which further implies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})-\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{n\Delta_{(k-1)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2}).

Combining Case 1 and Case 2 together, we have

\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\leq\frac{n\Delta_{(k-1)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2}).

Now we consider the case when
j>1
. Based on the above inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-j,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{n\Delta_{(k-1)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\frac{n\Delta_{(k-2)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-2,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\cdots+\frac{n\Delta_{(k-j)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right)+j\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{n\Delta_{(k-1)}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{nj}v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\frac{n\Delta_{(k-2)}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{nj}v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\cdots+\frac{n\Delta_{(k-j)}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{nj}v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right)+j\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{j}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{nj}}}\frac{n\Delta_{(k-j)}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-j,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+j\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2}).

This concludes the proof for Lemma
D.4
. ∎

D.5
Proof of Lemma
D.5

Now we prove Lemma
D.5
, which appears later in Appendix
D.7
.
We start by providing a lower bound for
\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}
for those
l\in[d]
with
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}
, where
Q_{k}
is defined in (
B.1
). We discuss the following two cases.

Case 1:

When
\partial_{l}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)m_{l,k,i}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_concentrate_v_k}}}{\geq}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{2})\right).

Case 2:

When
\partial_{l}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)m_{l,k,i}<0
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_concentrate_v_k}}}{\geq}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}.

Let
i+:=\{\,i\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}:\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\,m_{l,k,i}\geq 0\,\}
and
i-:=\{\,i\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}:\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\,m_{l,k,i}<0\,\}
.
Accordingly, we write
\sum_{i+}(\cdot):=\sum_{i\in i+}(\cdot)
and
\sum_{i-}(\cdot):=\sum_{i\in i-}(\cdot)
.
Combining
Case 1
and
Case 2
together, we have the following results for
l
with
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i+}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}+\sum_{i-}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i+}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(1-(1-\beta_{2})n(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{2})\right)+\sum_{i-}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}+\sum_{i+}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(-(1-\beta_{2})n(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{2})\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i-}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\left|m_{l,k,i}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_f_over_v} and \ref{lemma_upper_m}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\left(C_{m,1}\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{C_{m,2}}{\sqrt{k}}\right),

where
\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})=(1-\beta_{2})n(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{2})+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1\right)
and
C_{m,1},C_{m,2}
are defined in Lemma
B.5
. We have

(II-2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{d\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\left(C_{m,1}\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{C_{m,2}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)-\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{4}}{k},

where

\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})=\frac{d}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot C_{m,1},\quad\widetilde{C}_{4}=\frac{d\eta_{0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot C_{m,2},

where
C_{m,1}
and
C_{m,2}
are defined in Lemma
B.5
. Note that
\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})
is a constant that approaches 0 when
\beta_{2}
approaches 1. This concludes the proof for (II-2). The proof of (II-3) follows the exact same procedure using Lemma
D.3
and we omit the proof for brevity. ∎

D.6
Proof of Lemma
D.6

Now we prove Lemma
D.6
, which appears later in Appendix
D.7
.
For those
l\in[d]
such that
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(m_{l,k,i}-\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-1,i}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-\beta_{1})\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,i})+\beta_{1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,i-1})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{i}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,0}}(x_{k,0})\right)\right]}_{\text{(III-1)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}(1-\beta_{1})\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,n-1})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,i+1})\right)\right]}_{\text{(III-2)}}.

We first provide a lower bound for (III-1).

(III-1)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-\beta_{1})\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})+\beta_{1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,0})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{i}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,0}}(x_{k,0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\right]\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_f_over_v}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-\beta_{1})\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})+\beta_{1}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i-1}}(x_{k,0})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{i}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,0}}(x_{k,0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\cdot\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-\beta_{1})(1+\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{i})\right]-2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})(1+\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-1})\right]-2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1}^{n})\right]-2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k},

where (i) follows by conditioning on the entire history up to
x_{k,0}
while excluding the fresh random permutation of indices within epoch
k
. Under
\mathbb{E}_{k}[\cdot]
, the quantities
\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})
and
v_{l,k,-1}
are treated as fixed, so we have

\mathbb{E}_{k}\!\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,-1}}}\,\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})\right]=\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,-1}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{k}[\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})].

Moreover, conditional on this history, for any fixed
i\in[n]
,
\tau_{k,i}
is uniformly distributed over
[n]
, hence

\mathbb{E}_{k}[\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k,i}}(x_{k,0})]=\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})
.

Now we bound (III-2) using a similar procedure.

(III-2)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,0})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\right]\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k-1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,0})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\left|(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,0})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right)\right|

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\right]\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_k-k-1}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,0})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot\frac{\Delta_{k-1}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right|+\cdots+\left|\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right|\right)

\displaystyle-\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\left|\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right|+\cdots+\left|\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right|\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\right]\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,n-1}}(x_{k-1,0})+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i+1}}(x_{k-1,0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot\Delta_{k-1}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})\cdot 2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{2}

\displaystyle-\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})n^{2}\left(n\Delta_{k}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\cdot\Delta_{k-1},

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\text{(III-2)}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\text{(III-2)}

\displaystyle-\left|\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\text{(III-2)}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|<Q_{k}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}\beta_{1}^{i+1}\left(1+\cdots+\beta_{1}^{n-i-2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-d\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})n^{2}\left(n\Delta_{k}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-d\Delta_{k-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})\cdot 2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{2}+2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{d\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})^{2}n^{2}Q_{k}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\geq}

\displaystyle-d\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})n^{2}\left(n\Delta_{k}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-d\Delta_{k-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})\cdot 2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{2}+2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{d\eta_{0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})^{2}n^{2}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right)}\frac{32(n+1)\Delta_{1}\left(\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n\right)}{k}

\displaystyle\overset{}{:=}

\displaystyle-d\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})n^{2}\left(n\Delta_{k}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-d\Delta_{k-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})\cdot 2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{2}+2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{8}}{k},

where (i) is due to
Lemma
D.1
and
B.4
, and (ii) is due to the property of random permutation:
\mathbb{E}_{k-1}(\partial_{l}f_{\tau_{k-1,i}}(x_{k-1,0}))=\partial_{l}f(x_{k-1,0})
for any
i\in[n]
. In (i), we are allowed to use Lemma
D.1
for
(k-1)
-th epoch since when
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}
,
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k-1,0})|
will also satisfy the condition of Lemma
D.1
automatically.

Combining (III-1) and (III-2) together, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\text{(III)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\left[\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}}\left(m_{l,k,i}-\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-1,i}\right)\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{(\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)\right]-\frac{d\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle-\frac{d\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})n^{2}\left(n\Delta_{k}+\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{d\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\Delta_{k-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})\cdot 2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{2}+2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{8}}{k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{\alpha,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right]-\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{9}}{k},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})n^{2}\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d^{2},

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{9}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}d\Delta_{1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}+n^{3}\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})+\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})\cdot 2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{2}+2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\right)\right)+\widetilde{C}_{8}.

This concludes the proof for Lemma
D.6
. ∎

D.7
Main Body of the Proof

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
3.3
. We apply the descent lemma on the auxiliary variable
z_{k}=\frac{x_{k,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k-1,0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}
.

\displaystyle f(z_{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f(z_{k})+\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k+1}-z_{k}\rangle+\frac{L}{2}\|z_{k+1}-z_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

(D.6)

We will provide an upper bound on
\frac{L}{2}\|z_{k+1}-z_{k}\|_{2}^{2}
and a lower bound on
\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle
. We start with
\frac{L}{2}\|z_{k+1}-z_{k}\|_{2}^{2}
.

\displaystyle\left\|z_{k+1}-z_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\left\|x_{k+1,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k,0}-x_{k,0}+\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k-1,0}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(D.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\left(\left\|x_{k+1,0}-x_{k,0}\right\|_{2}+\left\|x_{k,0}-x_{k-1,0}\right\|_{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\left(\left\|x_{k+1,0}-x_{k,n-1}+x_{k,n-1}-x_{k,n-2}+\cdots+x_{k,1}-x_{k,0}\right\|_{2}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left\|x_{k,0}-x_{k-1,n-1}+x_{k-1,n-1}-\cdots+x_{k-1,1}-x_{k-1,0}\right\|_{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:m_over_v}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{d}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(n\eta_{k}+n\eta_{k-1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{d}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)^{2}\cdot\frac{(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}n^{2}\eta_{0}^{2}}{k}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{C}_{1}}{k},

where (i) is due to
\frac{1}{\sqrt{k-1}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{{\sqrt{k}}}
when
k\geq 2
. We now provide a lower bound on
\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle
.

\displaystyle\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),\ \frac{x_{k,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k-1,0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}-\frac{x_{k+1,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k,0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left\langle\nabla f(z_{k})-\nabla f(x_{k,0}),\ x_{k,0}-x_{k+1,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k-1,0}-x_{k,0})\right\rangle}_{\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left\langle\nabla f(x_{k,0}),\ x_{k,0}-x_{k+1,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k-1,0}-x_{k,0})\right\rangle}_{\text{(II)}}.

We first prove a lower bound for (I):

(I)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\|\nabla f(z_{k})-\nabla f(x_{k,0})\|_{2}\|x_{k,0}-x_{k+1,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k-1,0}-x_{k,0})\|_{2}

(D.8)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{L}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\|z_{k}-x_{k,0}\|_{2}\|x_{k,0}-x_{k+1,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k-1,0}-x_{k,0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{L}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\cdot\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\cdot\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{nd}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\cdot\eta_{k}\cdot\|x_{k,0}-x_{k+1,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k-1,0}-x_{k,0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{L}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\cdot\left(\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\cdot\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{nd}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(n+\sqrt{2}n\beta_{1}^{n}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{k},

where (i) is due to

\displaystyle\left\|z_{k}-x_{k,0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{x_{k,0}-\beta_{1}^{n}x_{k-1,0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}-x_{k,0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}(x_{k,0}-x_{k-1,0})}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_delta}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\cdot\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}nd}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\cdot\eta_{k}.

We now provide a lower bound on (II), which is more involved.

(II)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\left[\eta_{k}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,i}}}\right]

\displaystyle=
