\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\left[\eta_{k}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}-\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,i}}}\right]\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\leq Q_{k}\right)}_{\text{(II-1)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}\right]\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)}_{\text{(II-2)}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,i}}}\right]\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)}_{\text{(II-3)}}.

We start with (II-1). Note that (II-1) imposes bounded gradient condition, and it can be simply bounded as follows:

(II-1)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:m_over_v}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{d}n\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}+\sqrt{2}\cdot\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)

(D.9)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{dQ_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}n\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{3}}{k}.

Now we handle (II-2) and (II-3), which focus on the case when the gradient is unbounded. We bound (II-2) and (II-3) in the following Lemma
D.5
.

Lemma D.5
.

Consider Algorithm
2
. Assume
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+1
. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

(II-2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,i}}}\right]\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)

(D.10)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\!\!\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{4}}{k},

where

\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})=\left(1-\beta_{2}\right){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}(\frac{8n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}+\frac{1}{2})+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}-1\right),\quad\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})=\frac{d}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot C_{m,1},\quad\widetilde{C}_{4}=\frac{d\eta_{0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot C_{m,2},

and
C_{m,1}
, and
C_{m,2}
are constants defined in Lemma
B.5
. Note that
\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})
is a constant that approaches 0 when
\beta_{2}
approaches 1. Similarly, we have

(II-3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!-\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left[\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k-1,i}}}\right]\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)

(D.11)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\!\!-\frac{\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{k-1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)

\displaystyle-\eta_{k-1}\tilde{\delta}_{4}(\beta_{2})\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{5}}{k},

where

\tilde{\delta}_{4}(\beta_{2})=\frac{d\beta_{1}^{n}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot\tilde{C}_{m,1},\quad\widetilde{C}_{5}=\frac{d\beta_{1}^{n}\eta_{0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{2}(\beta_{2})2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot\tilde{C}_{m,2},

and
\tilde{C}_{m,1}
, and
\tilde{C}_{m,2}
are constants defined in Lemma
B.5
.

The proof of Lemma
D.5
is shown in Appendix
D.5
. Combining
(II-1)
,
(II-2)
, and
(II-3)
together, we have the following lower bound for (II):

(II)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{(II-1)}+\text{(II-2)}+\text{(II-3)}

(D.12)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\eqref{eq_II-1}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{3}}{k}+\text{(II-2)}+\text{(II-3)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma_II_2}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}-\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)}_{\text{(III)}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(\eta_{k-1}-\eta_{k}\right)\beta_{1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left|\frac{\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right|\left|m_{l,k-1,i}\right|

\displaystyle-\left(\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})+\eta_{k-1}\tilde{\delta}_{4}(\beta_{2})\right)\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{3}+\widetilde{C}_{4}+\widetilde{C}_{5}}{k}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}-\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)}_{\text{(III)}}

\displaystyle-\frac{d\beta_{1}^{\frac{n}{2}}4n\sqrt{n}\eta_{0}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})k}\left(C_{m,1}\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)+\frac{C_{m,2}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})+\eta_{k-1}\tilde{\delta}_{4}(\beta_{2})\right)\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{3}+\widetilde{C}_{4}+\widetilde{C}_{5}}{k}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\geq}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\eta_{k}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\partial_{l}f(x_{k,0})\frac{m_{l,k,i}-\beta_{1}^{n}m_{l,k-1,i}}{\sqrt{v_{l,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\mathbb{I}\left(\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k,0})|\geq Q_{k}\right)}_{\text{(III)}}

\displaystyle-\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{6}}{k}\left(|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{7}}{k},

where

\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})=\tilde{\delta}_{3}(\beta_{2})+\sqrt{2}\tilde{\delta}_{4}(\beta_{2}),\quad\widetilde{C}_{6}:=\frac{d\beta_{1}^{\frac{n}{2}}4n\sqrt{n}\eta_{0}C_{m,1}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})},\quad\widetilde{C}_{7}=\widetilde{C}_{3}+\widetilde{C}_{4}+\widetilde{C}_{5}+\frac{d\beta_{1}^{\frac{n}{2}}4n\sqrt{n}\eta_{0}C_{m,2}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{n})}.

(i) is due to Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
B.5
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{k-1}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq\frac{2}{k}
for
k\geq 2
.

We now take conditional expectations
\mathbb{E}_{k}[\cdot]
and then
\mathbb{E}_{k-1}[\cdot]
and bound (III) in the following lemma.

Lemma D.6
.

Consider Algorithm
2
. Assume
k\geq\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{n\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+1
. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{L,D_{0},D_{1}}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
0\leq\beta_{1}<\sqrt{\beta_{2}}<1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\text{(III)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{\alpha,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right]-\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{9}}{k},

(D.13)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})n^{2}\sqrt{D_{1}}\sqrt{n}d^{2}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{8}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d\eta_{0}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\cdot 32(n+1)\Delta_{1}\left(\lceil\frac{\log(1/2)}{\log\beta_{2}}\rceil+n\right)\cdot\frac{(1-\beta_{1})^{2}n^{2}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{9}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}d\Delta_{1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{n}}\left(2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}+n^{3}\tilde{\delta}_{1}(\beta_{2})+\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot(1-\beta_{1})\cdot 2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{2}+2\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_{2}^{n}}}\cdot n^{3}\right)\right)+\widetilde{C}_{8}.

The proof of Lemma
D.6
is shown in Appendix
D.6
. Combining all the results from (I) to (III), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\mathbb{E}_{k}\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq_lower_bound_I},\eqref{eq_lower_bound_II},\eqref{eq_lower_bound_III}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}}{k}}_{\text{from (I)}}\underbrace{-\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{6}}{k}\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{7}}{k}}_{\text{from (II)}}

(D.14)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\eta_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{\alpha,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right]-\eta_{k}\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{9}}{k}}_{\text{from (III)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{\sqrt{v_{\alpha,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}\right]-\eta_{k}\left(\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})+\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})\right)\left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\right|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{6}}{k}\ \left(\mathbb{E}_{k-1}|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|+\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}\right)-\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}+\widetilde{C}_{7}+\widetilde{C}_{9}}{k}.

By taking expectations of both sides of (
D.14
) and repeating the steps used to derive (
6.44
), we arrive at the following inequality:
When

\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})+\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})\leq\frac{1}{4d\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}
, which can be achieved by setting
1-\beta_{2}=\mathcal{O}((1-\beta_{1}^{n})/n^{5.5})
, and
k
is large enough such that
\frac{\tilde{C}_{6}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{4d\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0}))^{2}}{n\sqrt{v_{\alpha,k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}}}-(\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})+\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})+\frac{\tilde{C}_{6}}{\eta_{0}\sqrt{k}})|\partial_{\alpha}f(x_{k,0})|\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k,0})\|_{2}^{2}}{d\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\|_{2}}{2d^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-(\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})+\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2}))\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}-\frac{\tilde{C}_{10}}{\sqrt{k}},

where
\tilde{C}_{10}=\frac{\tilde{C}_{6}}{\eta_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}+\frac{32\Delta_{1}^{2}}{(1-\beta_{2})^{2}D_{1}d^{2}\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}}+\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Delta_{1}}{(1-\beta_{2})D_{1}nd^{2}\sqrt{5}}+(\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})+\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2})+\frac{\tilde{C}_{6}}{\eta_{0}})\frac{4\sqrt{2n}\Delta_{1}}{(1-\beta_{2})d\sqrt{D_{1}}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle\nabla f(z_{k}),z_{k}-z_{k+1}\rangle\geq\eta_{k}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k,0})\|_{2}^{2}}{d\sqrt{5D_{0}nd}},\frac{\|\nabla f\left(x_{k,0}\right)\|_{2}}{2d^{2}\sqrt{5D_{1}n}}\right\}\right]-\tilde{\delta}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}-\frac{\tilde{C}}{\sqrt{k}}\right\},

where
\tilde{\delta}(\beta_{2})=(\tilde{\delta}_{5}(\beta_{2})+\tilde{\delta}_{6}(\beta_{2}))\sqrt{\frac{1}{D_{1}d}}
,

\tilde{C}=\tilde{C}_{10}+\frac{\tilde{C}_{6}\sqrt{\frac{D_{0}}{D_{1}d}}+\tilde{C}_{2}+\tilde{C}_{7}+\tilde{C}_{9}}{\eta_{0}}.

Following the same procedure as in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{k\in[1,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k,0})\|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{D_{0}}},\frac{\|\nabla f(x_{k,0})\|_{2}}{2\sqrt{dD_{1}}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tilde{\delta}(\beta_{2})\sqrt{D_{0}}).

Finally, when
D_{1}=0
, i.e., when the bounded 2nd-order moment condition holds, we can arrive at a similar conclusion subject to some changes in the constant terms. The proof under
D_{1}=0
is strictly simpler than the current proof, as it reduces to the bounded gradient case. We conclude the proof for Theorem
3.3
. ∎

Appendix E
Discussion on Bias Correction Terms and Non-Zero
\epsilon

In the above analysis, we focus on Adam without bias correction terms and consider
\epsilon=0
(
\epsilon
is the hyperparameter for numerical stability in Algorithm
1
and
2
).
For completeness, we now briefly discuss how to incorporate the bias correction terms and non-zero
\epsilon
into our analysis above. Based on the current convergence proof, we only require several additional simple changes. We will first discuss non-zero
\epsilon
and then discuss bias correction terms. We will use the notations for Algorithm
1
but all the arguments also hold for Algorithm
2
.

Adam with non-zero
\epsilon
:

In our current analysis, we consider
\epsilon=0
. In practice,
\epsilon
is often set to be a small positive number such as
10^{-8}
.
Proving convergence with
\epsilon>0

is strictly simpler.
It only requires a few simple changes based on the current proof. We explain as below.

When
\epsilon\neq 0
, the new 2nd-order momentum becomes
\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}:=\sqrt{v_{l,k}}+\epsilon
. With this change, the new proof can be conducted with the following minor changes over the current proof in in Section
6

First of all, we make the following slight changes in the current proof:

•

First, for Lemma
B.3
, we change the condition from
|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\geq\frac{8\sqrt{2}j\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}
to
|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\geq\max\left(1,\frac{8\sqrt{2}j\Delta_{1}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)
. The conclusion of Lemma
B.3
is unaffected and remains unchanged.

•

Second, based on the new version of Lemma
B.3
, we change the condition of Lemma
4.1
from
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\geq R_{k}
to
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\geq\max\left(1,R_{k}\right)
. The conclusion of Lemma
4.1
is unaffected and remains unchanged.

•

We define the constant
\hat{Q}_{k}=\max\left(1,Q_{k}\right)
.

Now, we re-state the two cases from the proof in Section
6
:

•

Case 1 (bounded gradient):
when
\max_{i}|\partial_{l}f_{i}(x_{k})|\leq\hat{Q}_{k}
, we consider two sub-cases: if
\hat{Q}_{k}=Q_{k}
, then changing from
\sqrt{v_{l,k}}
to
\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}
does not affect the proof since it does not change the result of (
B.5
). In particular, the derivation in the paragraph “
Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(c)
” still holds. If
\hat{Q}_{k}=1
, then the gradient is bounded by the constant 1. This is equivalent to analyzing Adam under bounded gradient condition with constant 1. In particular, we have:

\sqrt{v_{l,k}}\leq\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}=\sqrt{v_{l,k}}+\epsilon\leq C,

where
C>0
is some constant that is independent of
\epsilon
.
Then, whenever we need an upper bound for
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}
, we use
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}
, and then we follow the same steps in the current proof.
Whenever we need a lower bound for
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}
, we use
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}\geq\frac{1}{C}
. This makes the proof strictly easier than the current proof since it reduces Adam to SGD. We omit the proof for brevity.

•

Case 2 (unbounded gradient):
the complement of Case 1, the
unbounded
partial gradient event
B_{l,k}^{c}
. In this case, we will have
\sqrt{v_{l,k}}
is lower bounded by a large constant in the same order as
\hat{Q}_{k}
, with high probability. This can be seen following the proof of Lemma
4.1
(from (
5.11
) to (
5.19
)). Since
\epsilon
is usually chosen to be significantly smaller than 1, we have
\sqrt{v_{l,k}}\approx\hat{Q}_{k}>\epsilon
. Therefore, we have the following relation with high probability:

\sqrt{v_{l,k}}\leq\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}=\sqrt{v_{l,k}}+\epsilon\leq 2\sqrt{v_{l,k}}.

Then, whenever we need an upper bound for
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}
, we use
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}
, and then we follow the same steps in the current proof.
Whenever we need a lower bound for
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}
, we use
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{l,k}}}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{v_{l,k}}}
, and then we follow the same steps in the current proof with minor changes on the constant. Finally, the tail probability can be controlled using the same procedure as in the current paragraph “
Lower bound of
\mathbb{E}(e)
”, and the tail bound vanishes exponentially as
\beta_{2}\rightarrow 1
.

The final convergence result will be independent of
\epsilon
.
The above arguments also hold for Algorithm
2
by changing the notation to
v_{k,0}
,
\hat{v}_{k,0}
,
\nabla f_{\tau_{k,0}}(x)
.

Adam with bias correction terms:

These bias correction terms are introduced by
(
48
)
. It has the following form:

x_{k+1}=x_{k}-\eta_{k}\frac{m_{k}/(1-\beta_{1}^{k})}{\sqrt{v_{k}/(1-\beta_{2}^{k})}+\epsilon}=x_{k}-\eta_{k}\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}^{k}}}{1-\beta_{1}^{k}}\frac{m_{k}}{\sqrt{v_{k}}+\epsilon\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2}^{k})}}:=x_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\frac{m_{k}}{\sqrt{v_{k}}+\hat{\epsilon}}

As shown above, bias correction terms can be implemented by changing the stepsize
\eta_{k}
into
\hat{\eta}_{k}=\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}^{k}}}{1-\beta_{1}^{k}}\eta_{k}=\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}^{k}}}{1-\beta_{1}^{k}}\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}
and changing
\epsilon
into
\hat{\epsilon}=\epsilon\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2}^{k})}
. We now explain how to include this change into our analysis.

We observe that the new stepsize
\hat{\eta}_{k}
is well bounded around the old stepsize
\eta_{k}
, i.e.,
\hat{\eta}_{k}\in[\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}\eta_{k},\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\eta_{k}]
. Therefore, to prove the convergence of Adam with
\hat{\eta}_{k}
, we add the following steps to the current proof.

•

Since
\hat{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon
, the previous analysis for
\epsilon
can be directly applied to
\hat{\epsilon}
.

•

Whenever we need an upper bound on
\hat{\eta}_{k}
, we use
\hat{\eta}_{k}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\eta_{k}
. Then we follow the original analysis with an extra constant
\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}
. It turns out we only need to change the constant
\Delta_{k}
in (
B.1
) into
\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\frac{\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{L\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}}}}
. The rest of the analysis remains the same.

•

Whenever we need a lower bound on
\hat{\eta}_{k}
, we use
\hat{\eta}_{k}\geq\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}\eta_{k}
. Then we follow the original analysis with an extra constant
\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}
. As such, we only need to change the constant terms in the final result. The rest of the analysis remains the same.

Appendix F
Experimental Settings

Here, we introduce our experimental settings.

•

Experiments on the non-realizable function used in Figure
3
(b).
Here, we state non-realizable function we used in Figure
3
(b). This example is restated from
(
75
, Appendix A.4)
.

f_{j}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(x-a)^{2}\text{ if }j=0\\
-0.1\left(x-\frac{10}{9}a\right)^{2}\text{ if }1\leq j\leq 9\end{array}\right.

(F.1)

We can see that
f(x)=\frac{1}{10}\sum_{j=0}^{9}f_{j}(x)=\frac{1}{10}\left(\frac{1}{10}x^{2}-\frac{1}{9}a^{2}\right)
is a convex function and
f(x)
is lower bounded by
-\frac{1}{90}a^{2}
. We used
a=10
in Figure
3
(b). Note that we have
D_{0}>0
for function (
F.1
).

•

Experiments on the counter-example (
3.1
).

We minimize function (
3.1
) using Algorithm
2
with cyclic order
f_{0}
,
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
and so on. We report the optimality gap
x-x^{*}
after 50k iterations, or equivalently
50000/n
epochs. We use
\epsilon=10^{-8}
for numerical stability. We use diminishing stepsize
\eta_{k}=0.1/\sqrt{k}
, where
k
is the index of epoch. Unless otherwise stated, this setting applies to all the other experiments on function (
3.1
).

•

MNIST
(
22
)
.
We use one-hidden-layer neural network with width =16. We set batch size =1, weight decay =0, stepsize =0.0001 and train for 20 epochs. We use
\epsilon=10^{-8}
for numerical stability.

•

CIFAR-10
(
49
)
.
We use ResNet-18
(
36
)
as the architecture. We choose batch size =16, weight decay =5e-4 , initial stepsize=1e-3. We use a stage-wise constant learning rate scheduling with a multiplicative factor of 0.1 on epoch 30, 60 and 90. We use
\epsilon=10^{-8}
for numerical stability.

For MNIST and CIFAR-10, larger batch size will bring similar pattern as that in Figure
1
, but
the phase transition will occur at some smaller
\beta_{2}
.
